33	Existing skills (`/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`, `/code-review-and-quality`) are reused as-is; no new skills authored in `agent-skills-shaper` as part of this initiative.
34	
35	## §6 Key results — draft for review
36	
37	Type 2 playbook default mix is **correctness + no-silent-failure + maintenance-burden**. KR3 is the slot under discussion (§6.4 below).
38	
39	### §6.1 KR1 (stretch) — Verifier-gated correctness survives a real complex ticket *(bet)*
40	
41	- **observable claim:** at least one complex unsliceable ticket bearing `flow:plan-implement-verify` completes a contract drain with verifier-gated Done and merges to main with no operator-required fix on PR review
42	- **baseline:** 0 (no verifier today; worker self-asserts Done per decision §1)
43	- **target:** ≥ 1 such ticket
44	- **measured over:** the initiative's lifetime (existence proof; grades on first hit)
45	- **how we'll know:** `drain-cycle grade <cycle-id>` extended to report `contract_pass_count` per cycle — counts entries where `flow_label=="plan-implement-verify"` AND `verifier_verdict=="pass"` AND `final_linear_state=="completed"`; manual confirmation that the corresponding PR merged with no operator-pushed fix commits
46	
47	*Layer 1: correctness · Layer 2: golden-path test*
48	
49	**Review notes:**
50	- Single-trial existence proof per user direction. Less ambitious than a rate-based KR but easier to grade cleanly and avoids gaming the threshold.
51	- "No operator-required fix on PR review" is the load-bearing modifier — without it, "completes a drain" is just "the agent didn't crash." Open question: is "no operator-required fix" verified manually each time, or do we want a stricter automated proxy (e.g. `gt log --behind=origin/main` showing zero post-drain commits to that branch from the operator)?
52	
53	### §6.2 KR2 (commit) — No silent Done transitions *(brake)*
54	
55	- **observable claim:** across all contract-enabled drains, zero issues reach Done in Linear without a matching verifier verdict and full skill-sequence trace in the run log
56	- **baseline:** silent self-assertion currently possible (decision §1's contract); count is unknown because the trace doesn't exist yet
57	- **target:** 0 silent Dones
58	- **measured over:** every contract-enabled cycle for the initiative's duration
59	- **how we'll know:** `drain-cycle grade` fails any entry where `final_linear_state=="completed"` AND (`skill_sequence_trace` absent OR `verifier_verdict` absent) — emits exit code ≠ 0 on any violation
60	
61	*Layer 1: maintenance · Layer 2: structural cap + revisit gate*
62	
63	**Review notes:**
64	- This is the property that supersedes decision §1: the worker's self-claim is no longer authoritative; the verifier verdict is.
65	- Implication for decision §1: needs explicit ADR-style supersession in `docs/design-decisions.md` (scoped as slice 12).
66	
67	### §6.3 KR3 — slot under discussion
68	
69	Two viable shapes. Pick A, B, or "both with a 4th KR" via feedback below.
70	
71	#### §6.3.A Option A — Instrumentation/foundation *(foundation, commit)*
72	
73	- **observable claim:** every contract-enabled drain emits a complete skill-sequence trace (planner verdict + AC checklist, implementer claims, verifier verdict, halt reason if applicable) in both the run log and the OTel trace
74	- **baseline:** skill-sequence trace doesn't exist in the run-log schema
75	- **target:** 100 % of contract-enabled entries carry the full trace
76	- **measured over:** first 10 contract-enabled drains
77	- **how we'll know:** jq-based schema check against `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*.json` fixture in CI; `drain-cycle grade` reports `trace_complete_rate`
78	
79	*Layer 1: discipline · Layer 2: artefact-exists + sections-complete*
80	
81	**Why this might be the right slot:** KR1 isn't gradable post-hoc without the instrumentation. The foundation KR is load-bearing for the bet KR; without it, the lagging correctness signal collapses into a vibe check.
82	
83	#### §6.3.B Option B — Maintenance-burden *(brake, commit)*
84	
85	- **observable claim:** the contract doesn't add measurable operator overhead per drained cycle — false-positive verifier halts and contract-related manual restarts stay below a tolerance
86	- **baseline:** 0 operator-touch events per cycle today (drain runs unattended)
87	- **target:** ≤ 2 operator-touch events per contract-enabled cycle (false-positive halts, manual restarts, contract-related re-runs)
88	- **measured over:** first 5 contract-enabled cycles
89	- **how we'll know:** new field in the run log (`operator_touch_events: [{kind, at, reason}]`) populated manually after each drain; `drain-cycle grade` sums them per cycle
90	
91	*Layer 1: maintenance · Layer 2: ???* (would conflict with KR2 on rule 5b — both maintenance dimension)
92	
93	**Why this might NOT work:** rule 5b ("KRs name distinct Layer 1 dimensions") blocks this — KR2 already claims the maintenance dimension. Option B is only viable if KR2's dimension is reclassified (e.g. as outcome) or if KR2 is dropped/replaced. Worth knowing before picking.
94	
95	#### §6.3.C Option C — Both (4 KRs total, with foundation as KR3 and maintenance as KR4)
96	
97	- KR1 (correctness, bet, stretch) — as §6.1
98	- KR2 (maintenance: no silent Done, brake, commit) — as §6.2
99	- KR3 (discipline: instrumentation, foundation, commit) — as §6.3.A
100	- KR4 (??? : maintenance: overhead, brake, commit) — as §6.3.B
101	
102	**Problem:** KR4 and KR2 still share Layer 1 dimension (both maintenance) and would fail rule 5b. To make Option C work, either reclassify one (e.g. KR2 as outcome — "the worker's Done claim is verified by an independent gate") or drop one of them. The skill caps KRs at 5 and warns against padding, so we should commit to a clean 3-KR mix unless a 4th dimension is genuinely load-bearing.
103	
104	#### §6.3 — Recommendation
105	
106	**Lean toward Option A (foundation).** Reasons:
107	1. KR1 isn't gradable without the trace — the foundation is structurally required.
108	2. KR2 already covers the no-silent-failure property that Type 2's playbook defaults to.
109	3. Maintenance-burden as a target can live in the **kill condition** (§8) rather than as a KR — "if the contract adds > 2 operator-touch events per cycle, kill it" — which is honest about it being a stopping condition rather than a thing we're tracking weekly.
110	
111	**Open question for review:** confirm A, or push back if you actually want B/C.
112	
113	## §7 Appetite
114	
115	**~12 issues — Medium-to-Large.**
116	
117	Indicative slice sketch (planning-and-task-breakdown produces the real list later; not a commitment to this exact decomposition):
118	
119	1. **Planner spike** — does a fresh-context planner produce useful AC checklists on 3 real tickets, including one "unsliceable" one? Output: written recommendation; decision to proceed/reshape/kill before slice 2.
120	2. **shape:design-doc** — architecture for the required-skill-sequence contract: label mechanism, prompt structure, halt behaviour, run-log schema, OTel attribute shape, decision §1 supersession path.
121	3. **`flow:` label mechanism** — Linear label group, parser, validation; mirrors `repo:` / `model:` resolution from decisions §5 and §7. Default = no flow (current behaviour).
122	4. **Run-log schema extension** — new top-level + per-entry fields for `skill_sequence_trace`, `planner_verdict`, `verifier_verdict`, `flow_label`, `halt_reason` if applicable. OTel span attributes extended in parallel.
123	5–6. **Worker integration: planner pre-step** — invoke `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` as first action when `flow:plan-implement-verify` is set; capture AC checklist; halt if planner can't produce one.
124	7–8. **Worker integration: verifier post-step** — invoke `/code-review-and-quality` against working-tree changes; parse verdict; gate Done transition.
125	9. **Halt-on-verifier-fail + revert path** — extends decision §8/§9 machinery: leaves worktree, halts cycle, names breach.
126	10. **`drain-cycle grade` extensions** — `contract_pass_count`, `trace_complete_rate`, silent-Done detection.
127	11. **Cost-impact handling** — measure per-flow token multiplier; add `flow:` overrides in `limits.yml` if cycle cap pressure shows up.
128	12. **`docs/design-decisions.md` §1 supersession** — write a successor decision explaining the verifier-gated Done contract; mark §1 superseded with link.
129	
130	## §8 Kill condition
131	
132	If, after running contract-enabled flows on **5+ complex tickets across 2 cycles**, the verifier produces **zero true-positive findings** (every halt was a false positive, and verifier-passed Dones don't visibly improve PR-review outcomes vs non-contract drains) — the planner+verifier overhead isn't earning its keep.
133	
134	Action on kill: revert the contract enforcement (the `flow:` label becomes a no-op or is removed); keep the instrumentation (skill-sequence trace stays in run log + OTel); record the negative result as a new decision in `docs/design-decisions.md`.
135	
136	**Open question:** is "operator-touch events per cycle > 2" (the §6.3.B target) also a kill trigger, or only a "tune the contract" trigger? If yes, append to this condition.
137	
138	## §9 Out of scope (explicitly)
139	
140	- **PR feedback loop on stacked Graphite PRs** — sibling initiative. Shares skill-as-primitive philosophy but has its own trigger problem and blast radius.
141	- **Parallel drains** — orthogonal; correctness-focused initiative has no reason to touch decision §8's serial-drain stance.
142	- **Authoring new skills** in `agent-skills-shaper` — initiative reuses existing skills; new-skill authoring would be a separate initiative if needed.
143	
144	## §10 Open review points (summary)
145	
146	1. **§6.3 — KR3 slot:** A (foundation), B (maintenance, blocked by rule 5b), or C (both, with 4 KRs, also blocked unless one dimension reclassified). Recommendation: **A**.
147	2. **§6.1 — KR1 grading:** is "no operator-required fix" verified manually, or via a stricter automated proxy (e.g. zero post-drain operator commits)?
148	3. **§6.2 — KR2 measurement window:** "every contract-enabled cycle for the initiative's duration" — fine, or should it cap (e.g. first 10 cycles) for grading discipline?
149	4. **§7 — appetite shape:** ~12 issues feels right; any slice you'd drop or split?
150	5. **§8 — kill condition:** should operator-touch volume be a kill trigger or only a tuning trigger?
151	6. **§9 — out-of-scope:** anything in here that should actually be in scope, or anything in-scope above that you'd push out?
152	
153	## §11 Next steps
154	
155	1. You leave inline feedback on this doc.
156	2. I revise §6 and any other affected sections.
157	3. Repeat until you confirm (Step 6 gate).
158	4. I run the 11-rule verification rubric (Step 6.5) and report any failures.
159	5. Once all 11 rules pass, I create the Linear project via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_project` with the synthesised body, status Planned.
160	6. Sibling work (the design-doc, slice 2 of §7) becomes the next phase.
161

  "new_string": "## §6 Goal and Key results\n\nThe KRs below are written in plain English — a non-pack-author should be able to grade either one in 30 seconds. Rubric annotations (Layer 1 / Layer 2 dimensions) live in italic audit-footers under each KR; the dimensions summary is at the end of §6.\n\n### §6.0 Goal\n\n**For** the operator running drain-cycle on complex tickets that can't be sliced further, **we want** a second agent to verify the produced code before the ticket is marked Done — **so that** incomplete or under-verified work doesn't silently land on `main`.\n\n### §6.1 KR1 (stretch) — Of the next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow, ≥ 3 ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits *(bet)*\n\n- **plain-English version:** when I run drain-cycle on a hard ticket I couldn't break into smaller pieces, a second agent reviews the produced code before the ticket is marked Done. The ship-rate-without-fixes is unmeasured because there's no record of which tickets went through which path.\n- **target:** ≥ 3 of the next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits.\n- **measured over:** the first 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow (~2 cycles at current cadence).\n- **how we'll grade it:** two new artefacts, both built as slice 10 in §7:\n  1. **Per-ticket grade template** — a markdown checklist filled in after each drain that used the new flow. Questions: did the code cover every stated AC? did the review catch any real issue? did the PR merge clean, or did I push fix commits? Stored at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md`, one file per graded ticket.\n  2. **`drain-cycle grade --flow=verify`** — extends the existing per-cycle grade command (which already reads `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*.json`); reads the per-ticket grade files and reports pass-count and pass-rate across a window of tickets.\n\n  **What \"new\" actually means here** (re c_b1b472): no new streaming logs or metrics are emitted — drain-cycle already writes a run-log JSON per invocation and (since commit 3eae2f5) OTel spans to Honeycomb. The new things are (a) **two new fields** added to each run-log entry — `flow` (which flow this drain used; `null` for the default flow) and `verifier_verdict` (the reviewer agent's pass/fail and findings); (b) **two new OTel span attributes** mirroring those fields on the `drain.issue` span; (c) **the per-ticket grade markdown** described above; (d) **the `--flow` subcommand** on the existing `drain-cycle grade`.\n\n*Layer 1: correctness · Layer 2: golden-path test*\n\n### §6.2 KR2 (commit) — Every ticket reaching Done in Linear under the new flow has a recorded second-agent review verdict in the run log; zero exceptions *(brake)*\n\n- **plain-English version:** the worker is no longer trusted to mark its own ticket Done. The contract is: a ticket using the new flow gets marked Done only if the run log shows a second-agent review ran and produced a verdict. This protects against the failure mode where the agent thinks it's finished but actually missed half the AC.\n- **baseline:** silent self-assertion currently possible (per decision §1's contract); count of past silent Dones is unknown because the recording fields don't exist yet.\n- **target:** 0 tickets reach Done in Linear under the new flow without a `verifier_verdict` recorded in the run log.\n- **measured over:** every cycle that runs the new flow, for the initiative's duration (cap at 10 cycles if the initiative runs long).\n- **how we'll grade it:** `drain-cycle grade` is extended to fail any entry where `flow == \"verify\"` AND `final_linear_state == \"completed\"` AND `verifier_verdict` is absent. Exit code ≠ 0 on any violation. A single violation in any cycle fails the KR — this is a brake, not a rate.\n\n*Layer 1: maintenance · Layer 2: structural cap + revisit gate*\n\n### §6.3 KR count — manual override taken\n\n**KR3 dropped per review** (c_a2a5ee). The instrumentation work it would have measured (per-agent step records in the run log and OTel trace) is folded into KR1's \"how we'll grade it\" as a prerequisite, and remains explicitly scoped as slice 4 of §7. Keeping it as a separate KR was padding given how tightly coupled it is to KR1's grading mechanism — you can't grade KR1 without the instrumentation, so KR1's how-we'll-know already mandates it.\n\nThis takes the operator's offered manual override of rule 4 (default ≥ 3 KRs). The 11-rule rubric at Step 6.5 will flag rule 4 as failed; the override note in this section is the documented justification.\n\n*Dimensions: correctness / maintenance — distinct per rule 5b. KR count = 2 (manual override per review c_a2a5ee — instrumentation work survives as slice 4 of §7, not as a separate KR).*"
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md",
  "old_string": "10. **Grading workflow** — (a) per-ticket grade template at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` matching KR1's questions; (b) `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` subcommand reading the templates and reporting pass-count / pass-rate across a ticket window; (c) silent-Done detection (KR2 grader): fail any run-log entry where `flow == \"verify\"` AND `final_linear_state == \"completed\"` AND `verifier_verdict` is absent.",
  "new_string": "10. **Grading workflow** — designed for low-friction operator confirmation rather than blank-page authorship (per round-2 review c_1d55a4):\n    1. **Per-ticket grade markdown template** at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` — checklist matching KR1's questions plus a `status:` field (`draft` / `confirmed`).\n    2. **Auto-draft on completion** — when a contract-enabled drain finishes, drain-cycle invokes a one-shot grading agent (single `claude -p` call) with the ticket body + run-log entry + diff, and writes a `status: draft` file at the template path with the agent's first-pass answers. Operator opens the file, reviews, edits where the agent got it wrong, flips `status: confirmed`. Less friction than authoring blank; still operator-owned at the moment of judgement.\n    3. **`drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` subcommand** — reads grade files with `status: confirmed`, reports pass-count and pass-rate across a ticket window; warns about un-confirmed drafts so they don't get forgotten.\n    4. **Silent-Done detection** (KR2 grader): fail any run-log entry where `flow == \"verify\"` AND `final_linear_state == \"completed\"` AND `verifier_verdict` is absent."
}
```

New sub-steps:\\n\\n1. **Template** — `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` with checklist + `status:` field (`draft` / `confirmed`).\\n2. **Auto-draft on drain completion** — drain-cycle invokes a one-shot grading agent (single `claude -p`) with ticket body + run-log entry + diff, writes the file with `status: draft` and the agent's first-pass answers. If this still feels like too much friction we can iterate further in round 3.\"},\n  {\"reply_to\": \"c_befa07\", \"body\": \"Clarified slice 1. The spike is **operator-driven manual investigation, not a drained ticket** — you invoke `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` interactively on 3 real tickets from a recent cycle (one well-formed, one ambiguous, one you'd call \\\"unsliceable\\\"). Output is `docs/planner-spike-findings.md` answering whether the skill produced useful AC checklists, whether the \\\"unsliceable\\\" ticket actually yielded one, and a proceed / reshape / kill decision before slice 2 (the design doc).\\n\\nWhy not a drained ticket: the spike is meta-work *about* what the worker should do, and we don't have the worker-side flow built yet to drain it through. It's the small upfront investment that the design doc (slice 2) then builds on. If `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` turns out to be the wrong skill for the role, the design doc names an alternative (existing skill, new skill, or bespoke prompt embedded in the worker's prompt).\"}\n]\nCRITJSON",

}

          "description": "Planner breaks the ticket into ordered sub-tasks the implementer then ticks off; sub-tasks live in a worktree file, not new Linear issues."
        },
        {
          "label": "Readiness gate",

Fair — let me lay out the full cast first, then ask the planner questions against that map.

## Roles in the worker session per ticket

The unit is one Linear ticket → one `claude -p` worker session in a worktree. Inside that session, the worker can invoke skills/sub-agents. Each of the roles below is a skill or sub-agent invocation, not a separate long-running process.

| # | Role | When in the lifecycle | What it does | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Worker** | Whole session | The `claude -p` shell. Reads the Linear ticket, implements code, commits, updates Linear state. The orchestrator inside the worktree. | **Exists today.** This is what drain-cycle already spawns. |
| 2 | **Planner** | Before any code is written | Reads the ticket, expands ACs / decomposes into sub-tasks / decides if the ticket is ready to implement. Hands its output to the implementer step. | **New, this initiative.** The four questions I asked were about how to shape this role. |
| 3 | **Implementer** | Middle of session | The actual code-writing pass. Today this is just "the worker keeps going after reading the ticket." With a planner in place, the implementer consumes the planner's output. | Exists today (it's the worker's default behaviour); becomes a named phase once the planner sits in front of it. |
| 4 | **Verifier** | After implementation, before Done | Fresh-context review of the diff against the ticket + planner output. Decides pass/fail. If fail, worker doesn't transition Linear to Done. | **New, this initiative.** Likely `/code-review-and-quality` invoked late. |
| 5 | **Grader** (auto-draft) | After the worker session finishes | Single one-shot `claude -p` invoked by drain-cycle (not inside the worker). Drafts the per-ticket grading template at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` with `status: draft`. | **New, this initiative.** Added in round 2 to reduce operator data-entry friction. |

## Outside the worker session

| Role | What it does | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **drain-cycle dispatcher** | Picks Linear cycle, spawns one worker per issue in its worktree, writes the run log, invokes the grader on completion. | Exists today. Extended in this initiative to read the `verify` label, record `flow` + `verifier_verdict` in the run log, and trigger the grader. |
| **`drain-cycle grade --flow=verify`** | CLI subcommand that reads `confirmed` grading templates and reports pass-rate across a ticket window. The KR1 measurement instrument. | **New, this initiative.** |
| **PR-feedback responder** | An agent that watches Graphite stacks for human review comments and addresses them autonomously. | **Out of scope for this initiative** — §9 lists it as a follow-on. |
| **Operator** (you) | Cycle planning; reviews PR stacks via Graphite; confirms grading templates (flips `draft` → `confirmed`); kills the initiative if KRs don't hold. | Human in the loop. |

## What's actually being built in this initiative

Three new things:

1. **The planner role** (#2 above) — design + implementation. Slice 1 was supposed to be the spike that decides its shape; you don't need the spike, hence my four questions.
2. **The verifier role** (#4 above) — wiring `/code-review-and-quality` into the worker's pre-Done gate.
3. **The grading instrumentation** (#5 + `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify`) — so we can actually measure whether 1+2 are working.

Plus the connective tissue: the `verify` Linear label, the new run-log fields (`flow`, `verifier_verdict`), matching OTel span attributes, the grading template format.

## Updated roster (post-clarification)

1. **Pre-Implementation Reviewer** *(replaces "planner")* — runs before any code. Two jobs:
   - **Sizing & path decision** — how many artefacts change? Big → split into multiple Graphite stacks. Small → single-shot, auto-mergeable to main. Carve-out: even small work goes down the human-review path if it requires human review AND won't block other queue jobs.
   - **AC sufficiency** — do the acceptance criteria cover all the behavioural changes being introduced? If not, halt or enrich.

2. **Implementer** — unchanged. Has `/code-review-and-quality` available as a sub-agent during implementation.

3. **Correctness Verifier** *(needs a better name — see Q1 below)* — strictly correctness, not code-review. Did the implementer build what the spec described? Were the goals reached?

4. **Pre-Human-Review Preparer** *(new role)* — walks each PR / Graphite stack PR after the Correctness Verifier passes, and rewrites the PR body to:
   - **What**
   - **Why**
   - **What the reviewer should focus on** (only the important/critical/risky bits; if nothing critical → route to auto-merge instead of human review)

5. **PR-Feedback Responder** *(moved IN scope)* — watches Graphite PRs for human review comments, addresses them autonomously, pushes the fix to the stack.

6. **Grader** — unchanged (auto-draft per-ticket template after worker finishes).

## Four things to decide before I redraft

**Q1 — Name for role #3.** "Verifier" is too close to code-review. Candidates: *Acceptance Verifier* / *Spec Adherence Reviewer* / *Outcome Verifier* / *Done-Gate*.

**Q2 — PR-Feedback Responder trigger.** Subscription-auth rules out GitHub Actions. Three plausible models:
- One-keystroke operator command (`drain-cycle respond <pr>`) — manual trigger, no daemon
- Lightweight polling daemon — runs in background, picks up new comments automatically
- Linear-ticket-driven — each round of human review becomes a Linear ticket, the next drain picks it up

Memory says you dislike always-on daemons, so I'd lean one-keystroke unless you want to reverse that.

**Q3 — Sizing rule for role #1.** Agent-judgement ("here's why this needs 3 stacks: A, B, C") with the reasoning logged, or a numeric rule (e.g. ≥ N files → split)? I'd default to agent-judgement v1 and add a threshold later if calibration drifts.

**Q4 — Appetite hit.** Adding the PR-Feedback Responder + Pre-Human-Review Preparer roles likely pushes the initiative from ~12 issues to ~18–22. That's still one initiative but a chunky one. Options:
- Keep it as one initiative, accept the size
- Split: ship the worker-side loop (roles 1–3, 6) as initiative A; PR-side loop (roles 4–5) as a follow-on initiative B, sequenced after A's KRs hold
- Some other split you have in mind

Pick a default for each (or tell me to pick); then I'll redraft §2 / §6 / §7 / §9 in one pass.

> AGENT

Answering Q2 first, then I'll read the current doc state and write the plan.

**Q2 clarification — webhooks vs subscriptions:**

No new subscription needed. The constraint is **where the agent runs**, not how the trigger fires. Three trigger options I had in mind:

| Trigger | What it needs | Subscription impact |
|---|---|---|
| **Polling** (operator's machine periodically `gh api`'s the PRs) | Just your GitHub CLI auth | None — uses subscription auth as today |
| **Webhook via tunnel** (smee.io / ngrok / Cloudflare Tunnel forwards GitHub webhook → localhost) | A persistent tunnel daemon + a local receiver | None — webhook itself is free on any GitHub repo; tunnel services have free tiers |
| **GitHub Action → Linear ticket** (Action receives the webhook, creates a Linear ticket; next drain picks it up) | A GitHub Action that only routes; no Claude calls inside the Action | None — Action does routing only; the actual agent work happens locally during the next drain, using your subscription |

The disqualifier on the original `claude-code-action` idea wasn't webhooks — it was that *running the agent itself* inside GitHub Actions would need API-key auth, which violates the subscription-only rule. Routing-only Actions don't have that problem.

So in practice the realistic v1 picks are **polling** (no daemon if you trigger drain manually; pulls a `gh api` query at drain-start) or **GitHub Action → Linear ticket** (no operator-side daemon at all; each comment-round becomes a queue entry). One-keystroke is also valid if you want full control over when the agent reacts.

I'll fold all three into §7 of the doc as a trigger sub-decision flagged in §10, with polling as the default unless you say otherwise.

1	# Draft Linear initiative — multi-agent collaboration for correctness
2	
3	**Status:** draft for review (Step 5 of `initiative-shape`). Linear project NOT created yet — pending user confirmation (Step 6) and the 11-rule verification rubric (Step 6.5).
4	
5	**How to use this doc:** leave inline feedback on any section. The KRs (especially KR3) and KR1 grading details are the primary review surfaces. Section IDs in headings make it easy to reference points.
6	
7	---
8	
9	## §1 Raw idea (Step 1)
10	
11	drain-cycle evolves from "drain a Linear cycle unattended" toward a **batch/unattended driver for an ecosystem of small composable primitives** (skills + MCPs), with Linear tickets as the work queue. The operator failure mode it addresses: **single complex tickets that can't be sliced further, where the implementing agent forgets details or under-verifies**. The bet is multi-agent collaboration across three surfaces — input refinement (pre-implementation ticket critique), output verification (fresh-context critic of the diff before Done), and PR feedback loop (autonomous response to human review on Graphite stacks). Skills + MCPs are composable primitives the worker invokes; ticket-driven and manual modes share the same toolkit. Subscription auth only (Claude Code / Codex CLI). Correctness > throughput.
12	
13	**Scope of this initiative:** input refinement + output verification + the contract mechanism that binds them. PR feedback loop is **explicitly deferred** to a sibling initiative — different trigger problem (when does the agent know there's new feedback?), different blast radius (amending pushed code).
14	
15	## §2 Problem-or-solution gate (Step 2)
16	
17	**Passes.** The problem is named and specific: *"single complex tickets that can't be sliced further, where the implementing agent forgets details or under-verifies."* The three surfaces are proposed solutions; the underlying operator-impacting failure is articulated.
18	
19	## §3 Project type (Step 2.5)
20	
21	**Type 2 — personal product, single-user.** Confirmed.
22	
23	Rationale: drain-cycle is described in `AGENTS.md` as "Python, single-user, personal product"; the artefact being evolved is the product itself; any new skills/MCPs are primitives drain-cycle invokes, not the headline deliverable; one user (you), so growth/MAU KRs are categorically inapplicable per Type 2 playbook.
24	
25	## §4 Negative outcome if not solved
26	
27	Complex Linear tickets that can't be sliced further get drained today, the worker self-asserts Done on exit (per design decision §1), and the produced diff routinely misses stated AC or skips thoroughness checks. The current contract has no answer for *"the agent thought it finished but missed half the requirements."* The operator finds out at PR review, manually re-invokes claude or fixes by hand, and the unattended-drain promise breaks on exactly the tickets where it matters most.
28	
29	## §5 Affected repos
30	
31	- **`drain-cycle`** — primary and only. Worker integration, run-log schema, `flow:` label mechanism, `drain-cycle grade` extensions, supersession of design decision §1.
32	
33	Existing skills (`/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`, `/code-review-and-quality`) are reused as-is; no new skills authored in `agent-skills-shaper` as part of this initiative.
34	
35	## §6 Goal and Key results
36	
37	The KRs below are written in plain English — a non-pack-author should be able to grade either one in 30 seconds. Rubric annotations (Layer 1 / Layer 2 dimensions) live in italic audit-footers under each KR; the dimensions summary is at the end of §6.
38	
39	### §6.0 Goal
40	
41	**For** the operator running drain-cycle on complex tickets that can't be sliced further, **we want** a second agent to verify the produced code before the ticket is marked Done — **so that** incomplete or under-verified work doesn't silently land on `main`.
42	
43	### §6.1 KR1 (stretch) — Of the next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow, ≥ 3 ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits *(bet)*
44	
45	- **plain-English version:** when I run drain-cycle on a hard ticket I couldn't break into smaller pieces, a second agent reviews the produced code before the ticket is marked Done. The bet is that this catches enough real problems that most PRs merge cleanly without me having to push fix commits.
46	- **baseline:** 0 of 0 — no second-agent review exists today; the worker marks the ticket Done itself (per design decision §1). The ship-rate-without-fixes is unmeasured because there's no record of which tickets went through which path.
47	- **target:** ≥ 3 of the next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits.
48	- **measured over:** the first 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow (~2 cycles at current cadence).
49	- **how we'll grade it:** two new artefacts, both built as slice 10 in §7:
50	  1. **Per-ticket grade template** — a markdown checklist filled in after each drain that used the new flow. Questions: did the code cover every stated AC? did the review catch any real issue? did the PR merge clean, or did I push fix commits? Stored at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md`, one file per graded ticket.
51	  2. **`drain-cycle grade --flow=verify`** — extends the existing per-cycle grade command (which already reads `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*.json`); reads the per-ticket grade files and reports pass-count and pass-rate across a window of tickets.
52	
53	  **What "new" actually means here** (re c_b1b472): no new streaming logs or metrics are emitted — drain-cycle already writes a run-log JSON per invocation and (since commit 3eae2f5) OTel spans to Honeycomb. The new things are (a) **two new fields** added to each run-log entry — `flow` (which flow this drain used; `null` for the default flow) and `verifier_verdict` (the reviewer agent's pass/fail and findings); (b) **two new OTel span attributes** mirroring those fields on the `drain.issue` span; (c) **the per-ticket grade markdown** described above; (d) **the `--flow` subcommand** on the existing `drain-cycle grade`.
54	
55	*Layer 1: correctness · Layer 2: golden-path test*
56	
57	### §6.2 KR2 (commit) — Every ticket reaching Done in Linear under the new flow has a recorded second-agent review verdict in the run log; zero exceptions *(brake)*
58	
59	- **plain-English version:** the worker is no longer trusted to mark its own ticket Done. The contract is: a ticket using the new flow gets marked Done only if the run log shows a second-agent review ran and produced a verdict. This protects against the failure mode where the agent thinks it's finished but actually missed half the AC.
60	- **baseline:** silent self-assertion currently possible (per decision §1's contract); count of past silent Dones is unknown because the recording fields don't exist yet.
61	- **target:** 0 tickets reach Done in Linear under the new flow without a `verifier_verdict` recorded in the run log.
62	- **measured over:** every cycle that runs the new flow, for the initiative's duration (cap at 10 cycles if the initiative runs long).
63	- **how we'll grade it:** `drain-cycle grade` is extended to fail any entry where `flow == "verify"` AND `final_linear_state == "completed"` AND `verifier_verdict` is absent. Exit code ≠ 0 on any violation. A single violation in any cycle fails the KR — this is a brake, not a rate.
64	
65	*Layer 1: maintenance · Layer 2: structural cap + revisit gate*
66	
67	### §6.3 KR count — manual override taken
68	
69	**KR3 dropped per review** (c_a2a5ee). The instrumentation work it would have measured (per-agent step records in the run log and OTel trace) is folded into KR1's "how we'll grade it" as a prerequisite, and remains explicitly scoped as slice 4 of §7. Keeping it as a separate KR was padding given how tightly coupled it is to KR1's grading mechanism — you can't grade KR1 without the instrumentation, so KR1's how-we'll-know already mandates it.
70	
71	This takes the operator's offered manual override of rule 4 (default ≥ 3 KRs). The 11-rule rubric at Step 6.5 will flag rule 4 as failed; the override note in this section is the documented justification.
72	
73	*Dimensions: correctness / maintenance — distinct per rule 5b. KR count = 2 (manual override per review c_a2a5ee — instrumentation work survives as slice 4 of §7, not as a separate KR).*
74	
75	## §7 Appetite
76	
77	**~12 issues — Medium-to-Large.**
78	
79	Indicative slice sketch (planning-and-task-breakdown produces the real list later; not a commitment to this exact decomposition):
80	
81	1. **Planner spike** — operator-driven manual investigation, not a drained ticket. Run `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` interactively on 3 real tickets from a recent cycle (one well-formed, one ambiguous, one you'd call "unsliceable"). Output: a written recommendation in `docs/planner-spike-findings.md` answering — did the skill produce useful AC checklists on all three? did it surface ambiguities the ticket missed? did the "unsliceable" one actually yield a checklist (i.e. was it slicing-resistant, or just under-decomposed)? Decision at the end: proceed / reshape / kill before slice 2. 82	2. **shape:design-doc** — architecture for the required-skill-sequence contract: label mechanism, prompt structure, halt behaviour, run-log schema, OTel attribute shape, decision §1 supersession path.
83	3. **`verify` opt-in label** — a single flat Linear label that opts a ticket into the new flow. Default (no label) is unchanged; ops/KTLO and simple bugfix tickets continue to drain as today. Generalised to a `flow:` label group only if/when a second flow is introduced — premature for v1 (per review c_dacf16). Parser added to `labels.py` alongside the existing `repo:` / `model:` resolution; no group machinery yet.
84	4. **Run-log schema + OTel attribute extension** *(prerequisite for KR1; absorbs former KR3 instrumentation)* — add two new fields per run-log entry: `flow` (string, e.g. `"verify"`, or `null` for default flow) and `verifier_verdict` (object: `{result, findings[], invoked_at}` or `null` if the flow didn't run a verifier). Add matching attributes on the `drain.issue` OTel span. Schema is additive — existing run logs and `grade` continue to work unchanged.
85	5–6. **Worker integration: planner pre-step** — when `verify` label is set, invoke `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` as the first action in the worker session; capture the AC checklist into the run log; halt if the planner can't produce one (covered by slice 9's halt machinery).
86	7–8. **Worker integration: verifier post-step** — when `verify` label is set, invoke `/code-review-and-quality` against working-tree changes before Done transition; record the verdict in `verifier_verdict`; gate the Linear Done transition on a pass.
87	9. **Halt-on-verifier-fail + revert path** — extends decision §8 / §9 machinery: on verifier fail, leave worktree, halt cycle, name the breach in `cycle_halt_reason`.
88	10. **Grading workflow** — designed for low-friction operator confirmation rather than blank-page authorship (per round-2 review c_1d55a4):
89	    1. **Per-ticket grade markdown template** at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` — checklist matching KR1's questions plus a `status:` field (`draft` / `confirmed`).
90	    2. **Auto-draft on completion** — when a contract-enabled drain finishes, drain-cycle invokes a one-shot grading agent (single `claude -p` call) with the ticket body + run-log entry + diff, and writes a `status: draft` file at the template path with the agent's first-pass answers. Operator opens the file, reviews, edits where the agent got it wrong, flips `status: confirmed`. Less friction than authoring blank; still operator-owned at the moment of judgement.
91	    3. **`drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` subcommand** — reads grade files with `status: confirmed`, reports pass-count and pass-rate across a ticket window; warns about un-confirmed drafts so they don't get forgotten.
92	    4. **Silent-Done detection** (KR2 grader): fail any run-log entry where `flow == "verify"` AND `final_linear_state == "completed"` AND `verifier_verdict` is absent.
93	11. **Cost-impact handling** — measure per-ticket token multiplier under the new flow; add `verify`-flow-specific overrides in `limits.yml` if cycle cap pressure shows up.
94	12. **`docs/design-decisions.md` §1 supersession** — write a successor decision explaining the verifier-gated Done contract; mark §1 as superseded with a link to the new entry.

97	
98	If, after running the new flow on **5+ hard tickets across 2 cycles**, the verifier produces **zero true-positive findings** (every verifier halt was a false positive, and verifier-passed Dones don't visibly improve PR-review outcomes vs default-flow drains) — the planner+verifier overhead isn't earning its keep.

100	Action on kill: revert the contract enforcement (the `verify` label becomes a no-op or is removed); keep the recording fields (`flow` and `verifier_verdict` stay in run log + OTel — they're cheap and useful for future investigation); record the negative result as a new decision in `docs/design-decisions.md`.

The Outcome Verifier is a *different* check: did the implementer build what the spec said, and were the stated outcomes reached?\n- Two more roles belong in this initiative: a **Pre-Human-Review Preparer** that ensures every PR has What / Why / Focus structure (and routes to auto-merge if nothing critical), and a **PR-Feedback Responder** that addresses human review comments on Graphite PRs autonomously.\n- One initiative, not two — but with explicit milestones so the bigger scope is tractable.\n\nThis plan describes the redraft for `docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md` that incorporates the reshape, then sends it back through crit (round 4).\n\n## Final roster (the redrafted doc will list this in §1 / §5)\n\nSix roles total. Three are new in this initiative; three are existing.\n\n| # | Role | Phase | New / Existing | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | **Pre-Implementation Reviewer** | Before code is written | **New** | Agent-judged sizing (small → single-shot auto-merge candidate; big → multiple Graphite stacks). Carve-out: even small work goes down human-review path if it requires human review AND won't block queue. Also checks AC sufficiency. |\n| 2 | **Implementer** | Middle | Existing | The worker session today. Has `/code-review-and-quality` available as an in-session sub-agent — *that* is the code-review, distinct from #3. |\n| 3 | **Outcome Verifier** | After implementation, before Done | **New** | Strictly: did the implementer build what the spec described, and were the outcomes reached? NOT code-review. Renamed from \"Verifier\" per Q1. |\n| 4 | **Pre-Human-Review Preparer** | After Outcome Verifier passes, before PR submission | **New** | Walks each PR (or Graphite stack PR), ensures body has What / Why / What-reviewer-should-focus-on. Routes to auto-merge if no critical focus items. |\n| 5 | **PR-Feedback Responder** | Post-submission, on new human review comments | **New** | Picks up Graphite review comments, addresses them, pushes fix to stack. Trigger model has three plausible variants (see open decisions below). |\n| 6 | **Grader** (auto-draft) | After worker session finishes | **New** | One-shot `claude -p` invoked by drain-cycle; drafts the per-ticket grading template at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` with `status: draft`. |\n\nOperator stays in the loop at three points: cycle planning, confirming grade templates (`draft` → `confirmed`), and reviewing PRs that route down the human-review path.\n\n## KR reshape\n\nThe override-down-to-2 from round 3 is no longer needed — the broader scope has three real, non-overlapping failure modes that each deserve a KR. Proposed new KR set (final wording in the redraft):\n\n- **KR1 — merge-clean rate on hard tickets** *(stretch, bet)*. Unchanged in spirit. ≥ 3 of next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits.\n- **KR2 — zero silent Dones** *(commit, brake)*. Every Done in Linear under the new flow has an Outcome Verifier verdict in the run log. Zero exceptions.\n- **KR3 — every PR submitted under the new flow has the What / Why / Focus body, or is correctly routed to auto-merge** *(commit, brake)*. Pre-Human-Review Preparer either produced the structured body, or marked the PR auto-mergeable.\n\nRule 4 (≥ 3 KRs) now passes naturally; the round-3 override note in §6.3 is deleted.\n\nThe PR-Feedback Responder doesn't get its own KR in v1 — its existence is structurally captured by KR1 (clean-merge rate would degrade if the responder doesn't address review feedback) and the Layer 1/2 dimensions stay balanced (correctness / maintenance / discipline) without a fourth KR.\n\n## Appetite & milestone restructure (this is the big change to §7)\n\nOld §7 was a flat list of 12 slices. New §7 reorganises around five milestones. Each milestone has a stop-the-line gate: if the milestone's gate fails, the initiative pauses for a kill/reshape decision before the next milestone starts. The total slice count grows from ~12 to ~18–22.\n\n- **M1 — Recording infrastructure.** `verify` label parser; run-log schema fields (`flow`, `outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, optional `responder_runs[]`); matching OTel attributes on `drain.issue`; `docs/design-decisions.md` §1 supersession. Gate: 3 verify-labelled hard tickets drained; verdicts recorded; halt path exercised at least once on a deliberate fail.\n- **M3 — PR-shape gate.** Pre-Human-Review Preparer; structured PR body writer; auto-merge routing logic; integration with Graphite stack output. Gate: 3 stacks emitted, all with What / Why / Focus bodies or auto-merge labels.\n- **M4 — PR-feedback loop.** PR-Feedback Responder skill/agent; trigger mechanism (defaulting to polling at drain-start, per Q2 conversation — open decision below); fix-push to stack; idempotency / dedup of already-addressed comments. The round-3 doc assumed reuse; the redefinition (correctness ≠ code-review) makes that less obvious.\n2. **PR-Feedback Responder trigger model** — three plausible options (polling at drain-start / GitHub Action routing to Linear ticket / one-keystroke `drain-cycle respond <pr>`). Default to Graphite-native since the rest of the PR workflow is Graphite.\n\n## Doc sections — what changes, what stays\n\n| Section | Change |\n|---|---|\n| §1 Raw idea | **Rewrite.** Lead with the six-role roster; webhook clarification summary; one-initiative scope statement. |\n| §2 Problem-or-solution gate | **Minor edit.** Still passes; update language so \"input refinement\" reads as \"Pre-Implementation Reviewer\" and add the PR-side failure mode. |\n| §3 Project type | **No change.** Still Type 2. |\n| §4 Negative outcome | **Extend.** Add the PR-side failure modes: PRs land without focus guidance, human review comments go unaddressed until operator manually re-invokes. |\n| §5 Affected repos | **Minor edit.** Note that `agent-skills-shaper` may receive one new skill (Outcome Verifier) if M2's open decision concludes that route. > DEVELOPER

write out all the skills that will likely be invoked, both by me (during planning) and then by the cycle-drain automation

i just want to audit what i have in place to make sure its still honouring the unix philosophy idea i had in mind

> AGENT

## Skill inventory — both invokers

The UNIX-philosophy check: every cell in the **Skill** column should be a single-purpose primitive callable from either invocation context. `[E]` = exists today, `[R]` = exists, needs repurposed prompt for this role, `[N]` = needs to be authored, `[—]` = not a skill (built-in worker behaviour or external CLI).

### Skills *you* invoke during operator-side planning & manual work

| Phase | Skill | Status | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Initiative shape | `/shape:initiative` | [E] | Six-field initiative gate + 11-rule rubric. |
| Idea triage | `/idea-triage` | [E] | Frames new problems before they become tickets. |
| Plan review | `/shape:plan-review` | [E] | Evaluates an existing plan against a rubric. |
| Task breakdown | `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` | [E] | Decomposes a ticket/spec into sub-tasks. |
| Spec writing | `/spec-driven-development` | [E] | Requirements + ACs before code. |
| Design doc | `/shape:design-doc` | [E] | Larger-than-ticket architecture artefact. |
| Doc/PR review | `/crit:crit` | [E] | Inline review of docs, plans, branch diffs, live URLs. |
| ADR | `/documentation-and-adrs` | [E] | Captures architectural decisions. |
| Code review (manual) | `/code-review-and-quality` | [E] | Five-axis review of branch/PR diff. |
| Debugging | `/debugging-and-error-recovery` | [E] | Reproduce → localize → fix → guard. |
| Implementation (manual) | `/incremental-implementation`, `/test-driven-development`, `/source-driven-development`, `/git-workflow-and-versioning`, `/shipping-and-launch` | [E] | Standard build/ship chain when you do the work yourself. |
| **Ticket assembly for drain queue** | `/spec-driven-development` + `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` | [E] | The two-skill combination you already use to produce a drain-ready ticket. |

### Skills the *drain-cycle worker* invokes per ticket

| Role / Phase | Skill | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1. Pre-Implementation Reviewer** — sizing + AC sufficiency | `/shape:plan-review` *with a new "ready-for-drain" prompt focus* | **[R]** | Reuses existing skill but its prompt today is for human reviewers; needs a prompt variant focused on (a) stack-shape decision and (b) AC-sufficiency check. Alternative: author `/shape:ready-for-drain` as a new skill. |
| **2a. Implementer** — code-writing pass | `/incremental-implementation` | [E] | Worker's main loop. |
| **2b. Implementer** — proof of behaviour | `/test-driven-development` | [E] | Run alongside 2a. |
| **2c. Implementer** — doc-verified API use | `/source-driven-development` | [E] | When touching external libs. |
| **2d. Implementer** — code-review sub-agent (in-session) | `/code-review-and-quality` | [E] | Already mandated by `linear-workflow.md` as pre-Done step in worker. |
| **2e. Implementer** — commits | `/git-workflow-and-versioning` | [E] | Stack-aware via Graphite MCP. |
| **2f. Implementer** — error recovery | `/debugging-and-error-recovery` | [E] | When tests fail mid-session. |
| **3. Outcome Verifier** — spec-adherence / outcome | new skill — working name `/shape:outcome-verify` | **[N]** | Genuinely new: takes ticket body + diff + run-log, asks "did this build what was specified and did the outcome land," writes structured verdict to run log. Distinct from `/code-review-and-quality` in input (spec + diff, not just diff) and output (verdict for run-log, not five-axis review). |
| **4. Pre-Human-Review Preparer** — PR body + auto-merge route | new skill — working name `/shape:pr-prepare` | **[N]** | Reads stack PRs from Graphite MCP; writes What / Why / Focus body; tags auto-merge eligible vs human-review-needed. |
| **5. PR-Feedback Responder** — address review comments | new skill — working name `/shape:pr-respond` | **[N]** | Reads PR review threads via Graphite MCP, makes the fix in the right worktree, pushes to stack. Trigger is drain-cycle-side (polling / Action / one-keystroke), but the skill itself is the same primitive regardless of trigger. |
| **6. Grader** (auto-draft) | new skill — working name `/shape:grade-draft` | **[N]** | One-shot: ticket + run-log + diff → fills the grade template with `status: draft`. Small enough it could be a hard-coded prompt in the worker; making it a skill keeps you able to invoke it manually too (UNIX-philosophy check). |

### Non-skill primitives the worker still uses

These aren't skills but they're the rest of the composition surface — included so the audit is complete.

| Primitive | What it is | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*` | Linear MCP — read/write issues, comments, status, labels, cycles | The work-queue interface. |
| `mcp__graphite__*` | Graphite MCP — manage stacked PRs | The PR-side interface. |
| `mcp__honeycomb__*` | Honeycomb MCP — observability | Operability surface; OTel spans already ship since `3eae2f5`. |
| `drain-cycle` CLI | Dispatcher: worktree creation, worker spawning, run-log writing, grader invocation | Orchestration, not a skill. |
| `gh` / `gt` CLIs | GitHub + Graphite shells | Called from inside skills (e.g. `/git-workflow-and-versioning`). |

## UNIX-philosophy audit — findings

**Strengths:**

1. **Shared toolkit confirmed.** Every skill in the worker table is *also* invokable by you manually — that's the design decision 10 symlink working as intended. The four `[N]` skills inherit this property because they'll be authored as standard skills, not drain-cycle-internal logic.
2. **Each skill is single-purpose.** No skill in either table is doing two unrelated things; the role-to-skill mapping is roughly 1:1, and where it's 2:1 (e.g. Implementer using five skills sequentially) it's because the *role* spans multiple phases, not because a skill is overloaded.
3. **Reuse over new authoring where defensible.** Five of the six worker roles use existing skills; only three slots need genuinely new skills.

**Warnings:**

1. **`/shape:plan-review` repurpose risk.** Marking Pre-Implementation Reviewer as `[R]` rather than `[N]` only works if the existing prompt cleanly accepts a variant focus. If the variant is more than ~20% of the prompt, this is `[N]` in disguise — author `/shape:ready-for-drain` and stop. Decide at M2-design time.
2. **Three new skills is a lot for one initiative.** `/shape:outcome-verify`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, `/shape:pr-respond`. Each is justified individually, but it does push against universal Rule B6 ("Generalise on the third occurrence, not the first"). The mitigation: each skill has at least two invocation paths (operator-manual + worker-automated), so the "third occurrence" rule is effectively pre-satisfied. Still worth being explicit about in the design doc.
3. **Grader skill (`/shape:grade-draft`) is borderline.** It's small enough it could be a hardcoded worker prompt. Making it a skill costs ~50 lines of skill scaffolding. Justification for skill-not-prompt: you said you want to invoke it manually on past tickets to retro-fill grades — that's the UNIX-philosophy tiebreaker.
4. **No skill for the sizing decision's *output*.** Pre-Implementation Reviewer decides "split into 3 stacks." That decision needs to land somewhere actionable — does it write to a worktree file, create Linear sub-tickets, or just instruct the implementer? Currently undefined; flag for M2 design doc.

**Counts:**

- Existing skills reused: 9
- Existing skills repurposed (prompt-variant): 1
- New skills authored: 3 (+ 1 borderline)
- New non-skill scaffolding (`verify` label parser, run-log fields, OTel attrs, grading template, `grade --flow=verify`): drain-cycle-internal, not skills.

{
  "plan": "# Plan — redraft `docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md` (round 4 reshape)\n\n## Context\n\nThe initiative as currently drafted (rounds 1–3) covers two worker-internal roles: a *planner* (pre-implementation) and a *verifier* (pre-Done). PR-side roles were explicitly out of scope. In conversation today, the operator clarified:\n\n- The \"planner\" framing was wrong — they already do that planning themselves; what they actually want before implementation is a **Pre-Implementation Reviewer** that decides *sizing/stack-shape* and checks *AC sufficiency*, not one that does the planning.\n- The \"verifier\" overlaps with code-review (which already happens via a sub-agent during implementation). The Outcome Verifier is a *different* check: did the implementer build what the spec said, and were the stated outcomes reached?\n- Two more roles belong in this initiative: a **Pre-Human-Review Preparer** that ensures every PR has What / Why / Focus structure (and routes to auto-merge if nothing critical), and a **PR-Feedback Responder** that addresses human review comments on Graphite PRs autonomously.\n- One initiative, not two — but with explicit milestones so the bigger scope is tractable.\n\nThis plan describes the redraft for `docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md` that incorporates the reshape, then sends it back through crit (round 4).\n\n## Final roster (the redrafted doc will list this in §1 / §5)\n\nSix roles total. Three are new in this initiative; three are existing.\n\n| # | Role | Phase | New / Existing | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | **Pre-Implementation Reviewer** | Before code is written | **New** | Agent-judged sizing (small → single-shot auto-merge candidate; big → multiple Graphite stacks). Carve-out: even small work goes down human-review path if it requires human review AND won't block queue. Also checks AC sufficiency. |\n| 2 | **Implementer** | Middle | Existing | The worker session today. Has `/code-review-and-quality` available as an in-session sub-agent — *that* is the code-review, distinct from #3. |\n| 3 | **Outcome Verifier** | After implementation, before Done | **New** | Strictly: did the implementer build what the spec described, and were the outcomes reached? NOT code-review. Renamed from \"Verifier\" per Q1. |\n| 4 | **Pre-Human-Review Preparer** | After Outcome Verifier passes, before PR submission | **New** | Walks each PR (or Graphite stack PR), ensures body has What / Why / What-reviewer-should-focus-on. Routes to auto-merge if no critical focus items. |\n| 5 | **PR-Feedback Responder** | Post-submission, on new human review comments | **New** | Picks up Graphite review comments, addresses them, pushes fix to stack. Trigger model has three plausible variants (see open decisions below). |\n| 6 | **Grader** (auto-draft) | After worker session finishes | **New** | One-shot `claude -p` invoked by drain-cycle; drafts the per-ticket grading template at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` with `status: draft`. |\n\nOperator stays in the loop at three points: cycle planning, confirming grade templates (`draft` → `confirmed`), and reviewing PRs that route down the human-review path.\n\n## KR reshape\n\nThe override-down-to-2 from round 3 is no longer needed — the broader scope has three real, non-overlapping failure modes that each deserve a KR. Proposed new KR set (final wording in the redraft):\n\n- **KR1 — merge-clean rate on hard tickets** *(stretch, bet)*. Unchanged in spirit. ≥ 3 of next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits.\n- **KR2 — zero silent Dones** *(commit, brake)*. Every Done in Linear under the new flow has an Outcome Verifier verdict in the run log. Zero exceptions.\n- **KR3 — every PR submitted under the new flow has the What / Why / Focus body, or is correctly routed to auto-merge** *(commit, brake)*. Pre-Human-Review Preparer either produced the structured body, or marked the PR auto-mergeable.\n\nRule 4 (≥ 3 KRs) now passes naturally; the round-3 override note in §6.3 is deleted.\n\nThe PR-Feedback Responder doesn't get its own KR in v1 — its existence is structurally captured by KR1 (clean-merge rate would degrade if the responder doesn't address review feedback) and the Layer 1/2 dimensions stay balanced (correctness / maintenance / discipline) without a fourth KR.\n\n## Appetite & milestone restructure (this is the big change to §7)\n\nOld §7 was a flat list of 12 slices. New §7 reorganises around five milestones. Each milestone has a stop-the-line gate: if the milestone's gate fails, the initiative pauses for a kill/reshape decision before the next milestone starts. The total slice count grows from ~12 to ~18–22.\n\n- **M1 — Recording infrastructure.** `verify` label parser; run-log schema fields (`flow`, `outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, optional `responder_runs[]`); matching OTel attributes on `drain.issue`; `docs/design-decisions.md` §1 supersession. Gate: an end-to-end \"verify\"-labelled ticket drains, records the new fields, no behaviour change yet.\n- **M2 — Worker-internal loop.** Pre-Implementation Reviewer (sizing + AC sufficiency), Implementer integration (no change beyond contract), Outcome Verifier (`/code-review-and-quality` is *not* the right skill for this — see open decision below), halt-on-fail path. Gate: 3 verify-labelled hard tickets drained; verdicts recorded; halt path exercised at least once on a deliberate fail.\n- **M3 — PR-shape gate.** Pre-Human-Review Preparer; structured PR body writer; auto-merge routing logic; integration with Graphite stack output. Gate: 3 stacks emitted, all with What / Why / Focus bodies or auto-merge labels.\n- **M4 — PR-feedback loop.** PR-Feedback Responder skill/agent; trigger mechanism (defaulting to polling at drain-start, per Q2 conversation — open decision below); fix-push to stack; idempotency / dedup of already-addressed comments. Gate: one real PR with operator feedback gets addressed by the responder end-to-end.\n- **M5 — Grading + cost + close-out.** Per-ticket grade template (with auto-draft from round 2); `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify`; silent-Done detection; cost-impact handling in `limits.yml`; post-launch review against KRs. Gate: KR grading runs; one of {ship cleanly, pause, kill} decided.\n\nThe plan file the worker eventually breaks this into (via `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`) will name the individual slices per milestone.\n\n## §9 (out of scope) — net change\n\n**Moves into scope:** PR feedback loop (M4); Pre-Human-Review Preparer (M3).\n\n**Stays out of scope:**\n- Parallel drains (still orthogonal; decision §8 unchanged).\n- Authoring new skills in `agent-skills-shaper` — *except* if M2's open decision below concludes the Outcome Verifier needs a new bespoke skill, in which case that one is allowed.\n\n**New out-of-scope items to add:**\n- Multi-operator / multi-tenant workflows (single-user product per §3).\n- Replacing Graphite itself (the PR-feedback work assumes the existing Graphite stack flow).\n\n## Open decisions to flag in new §10 (round 4)\n\nThese are the genuinely open ones the operator hasn't pinned yet. Each will be a numbered point in §10:\n\n1. **Outcome Verifier skill identity** — is this `/code-review-and-quality` reused with a different prompt focus, or a new skill (e.g. `/shape:outcome-verify`) authored as part of M2? Default in the redraft will be *polling at drain-start* per operator's daemon-aversion, with the other two flagged. The webhook clarification (no new subscription needed; constraint is *where the agent runs*) is now in conversation memory and will be summarised in §1.\n3. **Sizing rule threshold** — per Q3, default to agent-judged + reasoning logged. Confirm there's no fallback numeric threshold (e.g. ≥ N files) wanted as a guardrail.\n4. **KR3 commit-vs-stretch** — currently proposed as commit/brake. Confirm or flip to stretch.\n5. **Milestone gates as kill points** — confirm gates are stop-the-line in both directions (proceed OR kill), not just \"review and continue.\"\n6. **Pre-Human-Review Preparer and auto-merge mechanism** — `gh pr merge --auto` (GitHub native) vs Graphite's merge queue (`gt submit --merge-when-ready`-style). Default to Graphite-native since the rest of the PR workflow is Graphite.\n\n## Doc sections — what changes, what stays\n\n| Section | Change |\n|---|---|\n| §1 Raw idea | **Rewrite.** Lead with the six-role roster; webhook clarification summary; one-initiative scope statement. |\n| §2 Problem-or-solution gate | **Minor edit.** Still passes; update language so \"input refinement\" reads as \"Pre-Implementation Reviewer\" and add the PR-side failure mode. |\n| §3 Project type | **No change.** Still Type 2. |\n| §4 Negative outcome | **Extend.** Add the PR-side failure modes: PRs land without focus guidance, human review comments go unaddressed until operator manually re-invokes. |\n| §5 Affected repos | **Minor edit.** Note that `agent-skills-shaper` may receive one new skill (Outcome Verifier) if M2's open decision concludes that route. Outcome Verifier** — spec-adherence / outcome | new skill — working name `/shape:outcome-verify` | **[N]** | Genuinely new: takes ticket body + diff + run-log, asks \"did this build what was specified and did the outcome land,\" writes structured verdict to run log. Distinct from `/code-review-and-quality` in input (spec + diff, not just diff) and output (verdict for run-log, not five-axis review). |\n| **4. Pre-Human-Review Preparer** — PR body + auto-merge route | new skill — working name `/shape:pr-prepare` | **[N]** | Reads stack PRs from Graphite MCP; writes What / Why / Focus body; tags auto-merge eligible vs human-review-needed. |\n| **5. PR-Feedback Responder** — address review comments | new skill — working name `/shape:pr-respond` | **[N]** | Reads PR review threads via Graphite MCP, makes the fix in the right worktree, pushes to stack. Trigger is drain-cycle-side (polling / Action / one-keystroke), but the skill itself is the same primitive regardless of trigger. |\n| **6. Grader** (auto-draft) | new skill — working name `/shape:grade-draft` | **[N]** | One-shot: ticket + run-log + diff → fills the grade template with `status: draft`. Small enough it could be a hard-coded prompt in the worker; making it a skill keeps you able to invoke it manually too (UNIX-philosophy check). Borderline. |\n\n### Non-skill primitives the worker still uses\n\nIncluded so the audit is complete — these aren't skills but are the rest of the composition surface.\n\n| Primitive | What it is | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*` | Linear MCP — read/write issues, comments, status, labels, cycles | The work-queue interface. |\n| `mcp__graphite__*` | Graphite MCP — manage stacked PRs | The PR-side interface. |\n| `mcp__honeycomb__*` | Honeycomb MCP — observability | Operability surface; OTel spans already ship since `3eae2f5`. |\n| `drain-cycle` CLI | Dispatcher: worktree creation, worker spawning, run-log writing, grader invocation | Orchestration, not a skill. |\n| `gh` / `gt` CLIs | GitHub + Graphite shells | Called from inside skills (e.g. `/git-workflow-and-versioning`). |\n\n### UNIX-philosophy audit — findings\n\n**Strengths:**\n\n1. **Shared toolkit confirmed.** Every skill in the worker table is also invokable by you manually — decision 10's symlink working as intended. The four `[N]` skills inherit this property because they'll be authored as standard skills, not drain-cycle-internal logic.\n2. **Each skill is single-purpose.** No skill in either table does two unrelated things; the role-to-skill mapping is roughly 1:1, and where it's many-to-one (e.g. Implementer using five skills sequentially) it's because the *role* spans multiple phases, not because a skill is overloaded.\n3. **Reuse over new authoring where defensible.** Five of the six worker roles use existing skills; only three slots need genuinely new skills (plus one borderline).\n\n**Warnings:**\n\n1. **`/shape:plan-review` repurpose risk.** Marking Pre-Implementation Reviewer as `[R]` rather than `[N]` only works if the existing prompt cleanly accepts a variant focus. If the variant ends up being more than ~20% of the prompt, this is `[N]` in disguise — author `/shape:ready-for-drain` and stop. Decide at M2 design time.\n2. **Three new skills is a lot for one initiative.** `/shape:outcome-verify`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, `/shape:pr-respond`. Each is justified individually, but it does push against universal Rule B6 (\"Generalise on the third occurrence, not the first\"). The mitigation: each skill has at least two invocation paths (operator-manual + worker-automated), so the \"third occurrence\" rule is effectively pre-satisfied. Still worth being explicit about in the design doc.\n3. **Grader skill (`/shape:grade-draft`) is borderline.** Small enough it could be a hardcoded worker prompt. ~50 lines of skill scaffolding cost. Justification for skill-not-prompt: you wanted to invoke it manually on past tickets to retro-fill grades — that's the UNIX-philosophy tiebreaker.\n4. **No skill for the sizing decision's *output*.** Pre-Implementation Reviewer decides \"split into 3 stacks.\" That decision needs to land somewhere actionable — does it write to a worktree file, create Linear sub-tickets, or just instruct the implementer? Currently undefined; flag for M2 design doc.\n\n**Counts:**\n\n- Existing skills reused: 9\n- Existing skills repurposed (prompt-variant): 1\n- New skills authored: 3 + 1 borderline\n- New non-skill scaffolding (`verify` label parser, run-log fields, OTel attrs, grading template, `grade --flow=verify`): drain-cycle-internal, not skills.\n\n### Open audit decisions (to fold into §10 of the redraft alongside existing open decisions)\n\nA. **Pre-Implementation Reviewer skill identity** — repurpose `/shape:plan-review` (cheap) or author `/shape:ready-for-drain` (cleaner separation). Default: prototype on `/shape:plan-review` during M2; promote to a new skill if the prompt-variant grows too large.\n\nB. **Grader skill vs hardcoded prompt** — `/shape:grade-draft` as a skill, or a worker-internal prompt block? Default: skill, because of the retro-fill use case you raised.\n\nC. **Three-new-skill budget** — confirm you're comfortable authoring three (plus one borderline) new skills as part of this initiative, or push some into a sibling skill-authoring initiative.\n\nD. **Where the sizing decision lands** — worktree file? Linear sub-tickets? Inline implementer prompt? Flag for M2 design doc; doesn't need answering now.\n",
  "planFilePath": "/Users/anton/.claude/plans/proud-sprouting-snail.md"
}
