{
  "plan": "# Plan — redraft `docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md` (round 4 reshape)\n\n## Context\n\nThe initiative as currently drafted (rounds 1–3) covers two worker-internal roles: a *planner* (pre-implementation) and a *verifier* (pre-Done). PR-side roles were explicitly out of scope. In conversation today, the operator clarified:\n\n- The \"planner\" framing was wrong — they already do that planning themselves; what they actually want before implementation is a **Pre-Implementation Reviewer** that decides *sizing/stack-shape* and checks *AC sufficiency*, not one that does the planning.\n- The \"verifier\" overlaps with code-review (which already happens via a sub-agent during implementation). The Outcome Verifier is a *different* check: did the implementer build what the spec said, and were the stated outcomes reached?\n- Two more roles belong in this initiative: a **Pre-Human-Review Preparer** that ensures every PR has What / Why / Focus structure (and routes to auto-merge if nothing critical), and a **PR-Feedback Responder** that addresses human review comments on Graphite PRs autonomously.\n- One initiative, not two — but with explicit milestones so the bigger scope is tractable.\n\nThis plan describes the redraft for `docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md` that incorporates the reshape, then sends it back through crit (round 4).\n\n## Final roster (the redrafted doc will list this in §1 / §5)\n\nSix roles total. Three are new in this initiative; three are existing.\n\n| # | Role | Phase | New / Existing | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | **Pre-Implementation Reviewer** | Before code is written | **New** | Agent-judged sizing (small → single-shot auto-merge candidate; big → multiple Graphite stacks). Carve-out: even small work goes down human-review path if it requires human review AND won't block queue. Also checks AC sufficiency. |\n| 2 | **Implementer** | Middle | Existing | The worker session today. Has `/code-review-and-quality` available as an in-session sub-agent — *that* is the code-review, distinct from #3. |\n| 3 | **Outcome Verifier** | After implementation, before Done | **New** | Strictly: did the implementer build what the spec described, and were the outcomes reached? NOT code-review. Renamed from \"Verifier\" per Q1. |\n| 4 | **Pre-Human-Review Preparer** | After Outcome Verifier passes, before PR submission | **New** | Walks each PR (or Graphite stack PR), ensures body has What / Why / What-reviewer-should-focus-on. Routes to auto-merge if no critical focus items. |\n| 5 | **PR-Feedback Responder** | Post-submission, on new human review comments | **New** | Picks up Graphite review comments, addresses them, pushes fix to stack. Trigger model has three plausible variants (see open decisions below). |\n| 6 | **Grader** (auto-draft) | After worker session finishes | **New** | One-shot `claude -p` invoked by drain-cycle; drafts the per-ticket grading template at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` with `status: draft`. |\n\nOperator stays in the loop at three points: cycle planning, confirming grade templates (`draft` → `confirmed`), and reviewing PRs that route down the human-review path.\n\n## KR reshape\n\nThe override-down-to-2 from round 3 is no longer needed — the broader scope has three real, non-overlapping failure modes that each deserve a KR. Proposed new KR set (final wording in the redraft):\n\n- **KR1 — merge-clean rate on hard tickets** *(stretch, bet)*. Unchanged in spirit. ≥ 3 of next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits.\n- **KR2 — zero silent Dones** *(commit, brake)*. Every Done in Linear under the new flow has an Outcome Verifier verdict in the run log. Zero exceptions.\n- **KR3 — every PR submitted under the new flow has the What / Why / Focus body, or is correctly routed to auto-merge** *(commit, brake)*. Pre-Human-Review Preparer either produced the structured body, or marked the PR auto-mergeable.\n\nRule 4 (≥ 3 KRs) now passes naturally; the round-3 override note in §6.3 is deleted.\n\nThe PR-Feedback Responder doesn't get its own KR in v1 — its existence is structurally captured by KR1 (clean-merge rate would degrade if the responder doesn't address review feedback) and the Layer 1/2 dimensions stay balanced (correctness / maintenance / discipline) without a fourth KR.\n\n## Appetite & milestone restructure (this is the big change to §7)\n\nOld §7 was a flat list of 12 slices. New §7 reorganises around five milestones. Each milestone has a stop-the-line gate: if the milestone's gate fails, the initiative pauses for a kill/reshape decision before the next milestone starts. The total slice count grows from ~12 to ~18–22.\n\n- **M1 — Recording infrastructure.** `verify` label parser; run-log schema fields (`flow`, `outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, optional `responder_runs[]`); matching OTel attributes on `drain.issue`; `docs/design-decisions.md` §1 supersession. Gate: an end-to-end \"verify\"-labelled ticket drains, records the new fields, no behaviour change yet.\n- **M2 — Worker-internal loop.** Pre-Implementation Reviewer (sizing + AC sufficiency), Implementer integration (no change beyond contract), Outcome Verifier (`/code-review-and-quality` is *not* the right skill for this — see open decision below), halt-on-fail path. Gate: 3 verify-labelled hard tickets drained; verdicts recorded; halt path exercised at least once on a deliberate fail.\n- **M3 — PR-shape gate.** Pre-Human-Review Preparer; structured PR body writer; auto-merge routing logic; integration with Graphite stack output. Gate: 3 stacks emitted, all with What / Why / Focus bodies or auto-merge labels.\n- **M4 — PR-feedback loop.** PR-Feedback Responder skill/agent; trigger mechanism (defaulting to polling at drain-start, per Q2 conversation — open decision below); fix-push to stack; idempotency / dedup of already-addressed comments. Gate: one real PR with operator feedback gets addressed by the responder end-to-end.\n- **M5 — Grading + cost + close-out.** Per-ticket grade template (with auto-draft from round 2); `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify`; silent-Done detection; cost-impact handling in `limits.yml`; post-launch review against KRs. Gate: KR grading runs; one of {ship cleanly, pause, kill} decided.\n\nThe plan file the worker eventually breaks this into (via `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`) will name the individual slices per milestone.\n\n## §9 (out of scope) — net change\n\n**Moves into scope:** PR feedback loop (M4); Pre-Human-Review Preparer (M3).\n\n**Stays out of scope:**\n- Parallel drains (still orthogonal; decision §8 unchanged).\n- Authoring new skills in `agent-skills-shaper` — *except* if M2's open decision below concludes the Outcome Verifier needs a new bespoke skill, in which case that one is allowed.\n\n**New out-of-scope items to add:**\n- Multi-operator / multi-tenant workflows (single-user product per §3).\n- Replacing Graphite itself (the PR-feedback work assumes the existing Graphite stack flow).\n\n## Open decisions to flag in new §10 (round 4)\n\nThese are the genuinely open ones the operator hasn't pinned yet. Each will be a numbered point in §10:\n\n1. **Outcome Verifier skill identity** — is this `/code-review-and-quality` reused with a different prompt focus, or a new skill (e.g. `/shape:outcome-verify`) authored as part of M2? The round-3 doc assumed reuse; the redefinition (correctness ≠ code-review) makes that less obvious.\n2. **PR-Feedback Responder trigger model** — three plausible options (polling at drain-start / GitHub Action routing to Linear ticket / one-keystroke `drain-cycle respond <pr>`). Default in the redraft will be *polling at drain-start* per operator's daemon-aversion, with the other two flagged. The webhook clarification (no new subscription needed; constraint is *where the agent runs*) is now in conversation memory and will be summarised in §1.\n3. **Sizing rule threshold** — per Q3, default to agent-judged + reasoning logged. Confirm there's no fallback numeric threshold (e.g. ≥ N files) wanted as a guardrail.\n4. **KR3 commit-vs-stretch** — currently proposed as commit/brake. Confirm or flip to stretch.\n5. **Milestone gates as kill points** — confirm gates are stop-the-line in both directions (proceed OR kill), not just \"review and continue.\"\n6. **Pre-Human-Review Preparer and auto-merge mechanism** — `gh pr merge --auto` (GitHub native) vs Graphite's merge queue (`gt submit --merge-when-ready`-style). Default to Graphite-native since the rest of the PR workflow is Graphite.\n\n## Doc sections — what changes, what stays\n\n| Section | Change |\n|---|---|\n| §1 Raw idea | **Rewrite.** Lead with the six-role roster; webhook clarification summary; one-initiative scope statement. |\n| §2 Problem-or-solution gate | **Minor edit.** Still passes; update language so \"input refinement\" reads as \"Pre-Implementation Reviewer\" and add the PR-side failure mode. |\n| §3 Project type | **No change.** Still Type 2. |\n| §4 Negative outcome | **Extend.** Add the PR-side failure modes: PRs land without focus guidance, human review comments go unaddressed until operator manually re-invokes. |\n| §5 Affected repos | **Minor edit.** Note that `agent-skills-shaper` may receive one new skill (Outcome Verifier) if M2's open decision concludes that route. Otherwise drain-cycle remains the only repo touched. |\n| §6 Goal + KRs | **Rewrite.** New Goal sentence covering both worker-internal and PR-side surfaces. Three KRs (KR1 ship-clean, KR2 zero silent Dones, KR3 PR body structure or auto-merge). Drop the round-3 override on rule 4. |\n| §7 Appetite | **Rewrite.** Move from flat 12-slice list to five-milestone structure (M1–M5) with gates. Total grows to ~18–22 issues. |\n| §8 Kill condition | **Edit.** Add per-milestone gate behaviour. Keep the existing 5-tickets-across-2-cycles whole-initiative kill as the M2 gate; extend with M3/M4-specific kill cues. |\n| §9 Out of scope | **Rewrite.** Per § \"§9 net change\" above. |\n| §10 Open review points | **Reset.** Round 1/2/3 marked resolved; new round-4 list per \"open decisions\" above. |\n| §11 Next steps | **No change.** |\n\n## Verification (how the operator validates the redraft)\n\n1. After ExitPlanMode + redraft, the operator re-opens `crit docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md` (round 4) and leaves inline comments on any of §1, §6, §7, §9, §10 they want adjusted further.\n2. Two grading sub-checks at gate time:\n   - Roster table in §1 matches the roster table in this plan (six rows, three new).\n   - The §7 milestones each have a written gate condition.\n3. Once round 4 is approved with zero new comments, the redraft moves to Step 6.5 of `initiative-shape` — the 11-rule rubric. Rule 4 should now pass on its own (3 KRs), so no override is needed.\n\n## Out of scope for this redraft pass\n\n- Authoring the Linear project — that happens at Step 7 after rubric passes.\n- Writing the design doc (M1 slice 2 in old §7 numbering) — that's the next initiative phase, not this redraft.\n- Implementing any of M1–M5 — execution-time work, not draft-time.\n\n---\n\n## Skill inventory & UNIX-philosophy audit\n\nAudit of which skills run at each phase, split by invoker, to check the design decision 10 toolkit-symlink premise still holds (every skill callable from both contexts).\n\nLegend: `[E]` exists today · `[R]` exists, refined for narrower focus · `[N]` new, must be authored · `[—]` not a skill (CLI / built-in / scaffolding).\n\n### The framing this audit checks against\n\nPer operator review (c_b76ab7), the working split is:\n\n- **Human** drives the **why** (initiative goal + KRs) and the **what** (the outcome-focused Linear issue — agent-assisted, but human-led).\n- **Agents** handle the **how** — implementation, verification, PR preparation, feedback response — via a specific set of skills/workflows the human defines.\n- The **how** can be invoked manually (operator at the keyboard) OR automatically (drain-cycle running the same skills in a worker session). The skills are the same primitives in both cases.\n\nThe audit below splits skills by *invocation context* (operator vs worker), and within the worker context shows how each skill maps to a `how`-step the human predefined.\n\n### Skills *you* invoke during operator-side planning & manual work\n\n| Phase | Skill | Status | What it does |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Initiative shape | `/shape:initiative` | [E] | Six-field initiative gate + 11-rule rubric. |\n| Idea triage | `/idea-triage` | [E] | Frames new problems before they become tickets. |\n| Plan review | `/shape:plan-review` | [E] | Evaluates an existing plan against a rubric. |\n| Task breakdown | `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` | [E] | Decomposes a ticket/spec into sub-tasks. |\n| Spec writing | `/spec-driven-development` | [E] | Requirements + ACs before code. |\n| Design doc | `/shape:design-doc` | [E] | Larger-than-ticket architecture artefact. |\n| Doc / PR review | `/crit:crit` | [E] | Inline review of docs, plans, branch diffs, live URLs. |\n| ADR | `/documentation-and-adrs` | [E] | Captures architectural decisions. |\n| Code review (manual) | `/code-review-and-quality` | [E] | Five-axis review of branch/PR diff. |\n| Debugging | `/debugging-and-error-recovery` | [E] | Reproduce → localize → fix → guard. |\n| Implementation (manual) | `/incremental-implementation`, `/test-driven-development`, `/source-driven-development`, `/git-workflow-and-versioning`, `/shipping-and-launch` | [E] | Standard build/ship chain when you do the work yourself. |\n| Ticket assembly for drain queue | `/spec-driven-development` + `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` | [E] | Two-skill combo that produces a drain-ready ticket. |\n\n### Skills the *drain-cycle worker* invokes per ticket\n\n| Role / Phase | Skill | Status | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **1. Pre-Implementation Reviewer** — sizing + AC sufficiency + breakdown | `/shape:outcome-tasks` *(refined `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`)* | **[R]** | Refines the existing task-breakdown skill to focus on one outcome-focused task (typically a Linear issue). Inputs: the issue body + its ACs. Outputs: enriched AC checklist, sizing/stack-shape decision (1 stack vs N), per-stack implementation task list. Pairs symmetrically with `/shape:outcome-verify` (row 3). `/shape:plan-review` is the wrong fit — it operates on whole design docs, not single outcome-focused tasks (c_b76ab7). |\n| **2a. Implementer** — code-writing pass | `/incremental-implementation` | [E] | Worker's main loop. |\n| **2b. Implementer** — proof of behaviour | `/test-driven-development` | [E] | Runs alongside 2a. |\n| **2c. Implementer** — doc-verified API use | `/source-driven-development` | [E] | When touching external libs. |\n| **2d. Implementer** — code-review sub-agent (in-session) | `/code-review-and-quality` | [E] | Already mandated by `linear-workflow.md` as pre-Done step in worker. |\n| **2e. Implementer** — commits | `/git-workflow-and-versioning` | [E] | Stack-aware via Graphite MCP. |\n| **2f. Implementer** — error recovery | `/debugging-and-error-recovery` | [E] | When tests fail mid-session. |\n| **3. Outcome Verifier** — spec-adherence / outcome | new skill — working name `/shape:outcome-verify` | **[N]** | Genuinely new: takes ticket body + diff + run-log, asks \"did this build what was specified and did the outcome land,\" writes structured verdict to run log. Distinct from `/code-review-and-quality` in input (spec + diff, not just diff) and output (verdict for run-log, not five-axis review). Pairs symmetrically with `/shape:outcome-tasks` (row 1) — one defines the outcome-shaped task list, the other verifies the outcome was delivered. |\n| **4. Pre-Human-Review Preparer** — PR body + auto-merge route | new skill — working name `/shape:pr-prepare` | **[N]** | Reads stack PRs from Graphite MCP; writes What / Why / Focus body; tags auto-merge eligible vs human-review-needed. |\n| **5. PR-Feedback Responder** — address review comments | new skill — working name `/shape:pr-respond` | **[N]** | Reads PR review threads via Graphite MCP, makes the fix in the right worktree, pushes to stack. Trigger is drain-cycle-side (polling / Action / one-keystroke), but the skill itself is the same primitive regardless of trigger. |\n| **6. Grader** (auto-draft) | `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` CLI subcommand | **[—]** | Per operator review (c_832b0f): this is a CLI command on drain-cycle, not a skill. One-shot internal prompt: ticket + run-log + diff → fills the grade template with `status: draft`. Operator can invoke it manually on past tickets to retro-fill grades. |\n\n### Non-skill primitives the worker still uses\n\nIncluded so the audit is complete — these aren't skills but are the rest of the composition surface.\n\n| Primitive | What it is | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*` | Linear MCP — read/write issues, comments, status, labels, cycles | The work-queue interface. |\n| `mcp__graphite__*` | Graphite MCP — manage stacked PRs | The PR-side interface. |\n| `mcp__honeycomb__*` | Honeycomb MCP — observability | Operability surface; OTel spans already ship since `3eae2f5`. |\n| `drain-cycle` CLI | Dispatcher: worktree creation, worker spawning, run-log writing, grader invocation | Orchestration, not a skill. |\n| `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` | New subcommand — drafts the per-ticket grade template after a drain finishes (or retro on a past issue) | Per c_832b0f, replaces what was previously sketched as `/shape:grade-draft`. |\n| `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` | KR-grading subcommand — reads confirmed grade templates, reports pass-rate, warns about un-confirmed drafts | Carried over from round-3 doc. |\n| `gh` / `gt` CLIs | GitHub + Graphite shells | Called from inside skills (e.g. `/git-workflow-and-versioning`). |\n\n### UNIX-philosophy audit — findings\n\n**Strengths:**\n\n1. **Shared toolkit confirmed.** Every skill in the worker table is also invokable by you manually — decision 10's symlink working as intended. The four `[N]` skills inherit this property because they'll be authored as standard skills, not drain-cycle-internal logic.\n2. **Each skill is single-purpose.** No skill in either table does two unrelated things; the role-to-skill mapping is roughly 1:1, and where it's many-to-one (e.g. Implementer using five skills sequentially) it's because the *role* spans multiple phases, not because a skill is overloaded.\n3. **Reuse over new authoring where defensible.** Five of the six worker roles use existing skills; only three slots need genuinely new skills (plus one borderline).\n\n**Warnings:**\n\n1. **`/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` refinement scope.** Refining the existing skill to focus on a single outcome-focused task is conceptually clean (c_b76ab7), but the existing skill is general-purpose; the refinement may need either (a) a narrower variant alongside the general one, or (b) a full reshape with the general behaviour deprecated. Decide at M2 design time. Rename to `/shape:outcome-tasks` is the operator's preference (c_a406e2).\n2. **Three new skills is a lot for one initiative.** `/shape:outcome-verify`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, `/shape:pr-respond`. Each is justified individually, but it does push against universal Rule B6 (\"Generalise on the third occurrence, not the first\"). The mitigation: each skill has at least two invocation paths (operator-manual + worker-automated), so the \"third occurrence\" rule is effectively pre-satisfied. Still worth being explicit about in the design doc.\n3. **No formal landing for the sizing decision's *output*.** `/shape:outcome-tasks` decides \"split into 3 stacks.\" That decision needs to land somewhere actionable — worktree file, Linear sub-tickets, or inline implementer prompt? Currently undefined; flag for M2 design doc.\n\n**Counts:**\n\n- Existing skills reused as-is: 9\n- Existing skills refined for narrower focus: 1 (`/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` → `/shape:outcome-tasks`)\n- New skills authored: 3 (`/shape:outcome-verify`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, `/shape:pr-respond`)\n- New drain-cycle CLI subcommands: 2 (`drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>`, `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify`)\n- New non-skill scaffolding (`verify` label parser, run-log fields, OTel attrs, grading template): drain-cycle-internal.\n\n### Open audit decisions (to fold into §10 of the redraft alongside existing open decisions)\n\nA. **`/shape:outcome-tasks` rename adoption** — confirm renaming the existing `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` skill to `/shape:outcome-tasks` for symmetric pairing with `/shape:outcome-verify`. Cost: cross-references in other repos and `agent-skills-shaper` may need updating. Default: adopt the rename.\n\nB. **Refinement style for `/shape:outcome-tasks`** — full reshape of the existing skill (deprecating the general-purpose behaviour), or a narrower variant alongside it? Flag for M2 design doc.\n\nC. **Three-new-skill budget** — confirm comfort with authoring three new skills (`/shape:outcome-verify`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, `/shape:pr-respond`) as part of this initiative, or push some into a sibling skill-authoring initiative.\n\nD. **Where the sizing decision lands** — worktree file? Linear sub-tickets? Inline implementer prompt? Flag for M2 design doc; doesn't need answering now.\n",

14	## Final roster (the redrafted doc will list this in §1 / §5)
15	
16	Six roles total. Three are new in this initiative; three are existing.
17	
18	| # | Role | Phase | New / Existing | Notes |
19	|---|---|---|---|---|
20	| 1 | **Pre-Implementation Reviewer** | Before code is written | **New** | Agent-judged sizing (small → single-shot auto-merge candidate; big → multiple Graphite stacks). Carve-out: even small work goes down human-review path if it requires human review AND won't block queue. Also checks AC sufficiency. |
21	| 2 | **Implementer** | Middle | Existing | The worker session today. Has `/code-review-and-quality` available as an in-session sub-agent — *that* is the code-review, distinct from #3. |
22	| 3 | **Outcome Verifier** | After implementation, before Done | **New** | Strictly: did the implementer build what the spec described, and were the outcomes reached? NOT code-review. Renamed from "Verifier" per Q1. |
23	| 4 | **Pre-Human-Review Preparer** | After Outcome Verifier passes, before PR submission | **New** | Walks each PR (or Graphite stack PR), ensures body has What / Why / What-reviewer-should-focus-on. Routes to auto-merge if no critical focus items. |
24	| 5 | **PR-Feedback Responder** | Post-submission, on new human review comments | **New** | Picks up Graphite review comments, addresses them, pushes fix to stack. Trigger model has three plausible variants (see open decisions below). |
25	| 6 | **Grader** (auto-draft) | After worker session finishes | **New** | One-shot `claude -p` invoked by drain-cycle; drafts the per-ticket grading template at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` with `status: draft`. |
26	
27	Operator stays in the loop at three points: cycle planning, confirming grade templates (`draft` → `confirmed`), and reviewing PRs that route down the human-review path.
28	
29	## KR reshape
30	
31	The override-down-to-2 from round 3 is no longer needed — the broader scope has three real, non-overlapping failure modes that each deserve a KR. Proposed new KR set (final wording in the redraft):
32	
33	- **KR1 — merge-clean rate on hard tickets** *(stretch, bet)*. Unchanged in spirit. ≥ 3 of next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits.
34	- **KR2 — zero silent Dones** *(commit, brake)*. Every Done in Linear under the new flow has an Outcome Verifier verdict in the run log. Zero exceptions.
35	- **KR3 — every PR submitted under the new flow has the What / Why / Focus body, or is correctly routed to auto-merge** *(commit, brake)*. Pre-Human-Review Preparer either produced the structured body, or marked the PR auto-mergeable.
36	
37	Rule 4 (≥ 3 KRs) now passes naturally; the round-3 override note in §6.3 is deleted.
38	
39	The PR-Feedback Responder doesn't get its own KR in v1 — its existence is structurally captured by KR1 (clean-merge rate would degrade if the responder doesn't address review feedback) and the Layer 1/2 dimensions stay balanced (correctness / maintenance / discipline) without a fourth KR.
40	
41	## Appetite & milestone restructure (this is the big change to §7)
42	
43	Old §7 was a flat list of 12 slices. New §7 reorganises around five milestones. Each milestone has a stop-the-line gate: if the milestone's gate fails, the initiative pauses for a kill/reshape decision before the next milestone starts. The total slice count grows from ~12 to ~18–22.
44	
45	- **M1 — Recording infrastructure.** `verify` label parser; run-log schema fields (`flow`, `outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, optional `responder_runs[]`); matching OTel attributes on `drain.issue`; `docs/design-decisions.md` §1 supersession. Gate: an end-to-end "verify"-labelled ticket drains, records the new fields, no behaviour change yet.
46	- **M2 — Worker-internal loop.** Pre-Implementation Reviewer (sizing + AC sufficiency), Implementer integration (no change beyond contract), Outcome Verifier (`/code-review-and-quality` is *not* the right skill for this — see open decision below), halt-on-fail path. Gate: 3 verify-labelled hard tickets drained; verdicts recorded; halt path exercised at least once on a deliberate fail.
47	- **M3 — PR-shape gate.** Pre-Human-Review Preparer; structured PR body writer; auto-merge routing logic; integration with Graphite stack output. Gate: 3 stacks emitted, all with What / Why / Focus bodies or auto-merge labels.
48	- **M4 — PR-feedback loop.** PR-Feedback Responder skill/agent; trigger mechanism (defaulting to polling at drain-start, per Q2 conversation — open decision below); fix-push to stack; idempotency / dedup of already-addressed comments. Gate: one real PR with operator feedback gets addressed by the responder end-to-end.
```

Gate: 3 verify-labelled hard tickets drained; verdicts recorded; halt path exercised at least once on a deliberate fail.
52	- **M3 — PR-shape gate.** Pre-Human-Review Preparer; structured PR body writer; auto-merge routing logic; integration with Graphite stack output. Gate: 3 stacks emitted, all with What / Why / Focus bodies or auto-merge labels.
53	- **M4 — PR-feedback loop.** PR-Feedback Responder skill/agent; trigger mechanism (defaulting to polling at drain-start, per Q2 conversation — open decision below); fix-push to stack; idempotency / dedup of already-addressed comments. Gate: one real PR with operator feedback gets addressed by the responder end-to-end.
54	- **M5 — Grading + cost + close-out.** Per-ticket grade template (with auto-draft from round 2); `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify`; silent-Done detection; cost-impact handling in `limits.yml`; post-launch review against KRs. Gate: KR grading runs; one of {ship cleanly, pause, kill} decided.
55	
56	The plan file the worker eventually breaks this into (via `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`) will name the individual slices per milestone.
57	
58	## §9 (out of scope) — net change
59	
60	**Moves into scope:** PR feedback loop (M4); Pre-Human-Review Preparer (M3).
61	
62	**Stays out of scope:**
63	- Parallel drains (still orthogonal; decision §8 unchanged).
64	- Authoring new skills in `agent-skills-shaper` — *except* if M2's open decision below concludes the Outcome Verifier needs a new bespoke skill, in which case that one is allowed.
65	
66	**New out-of-scope items to add:**
67	- Multi-operator / multi-tenant workflows (single-user product per §3).
68	- Replacing Graphite itself (the PR-feedback work assumes the existing Graphite stack flow).
69	
70	## Open decisions to flag in new §10 (round 4)
71	
72	These are the genuinely open ones the operator hasn't pinned yet. Each will be a numbered point in §10:
73	
74	1. **Outcome Verifier skill identity** — is this `/code-review-and-quality` reused with a different prompt focus, or a new skill (e.g. `/shape:outcome-verify`) authored as part of M2? The round-3 doc assumed reuse; the redefinition (correctness ≠ code-review) makes that less obvious.
75	2. **PR-Feedback Responder trigger model** — three plausible options (polling at drain-start / GitHub Action routing to Linear ticket / one-keystroke `drain-cycle respond <pr>`). Default in the redraft will be *polling at drain-start* per operator's daemon-aversion, with the other two flagged. The webhook clarification (no new subscription needed; constraint is *where the agent runs*) is now in conversation memory and will be summarised in §1.
76	3. **Sizing rule threshold** — per Q3, default to agent-judged + reasoning logged. Confirm there's no fallback numeric threshold (e.g. ≥ N files) wanted as a guardrail.
77	4. **KR3 commit-vs-stretch** — currently proposed as commit/brake. Confirm or flip to stretch.
78	5. **Milestone gates as kill points** — confirm gates are stop-the-line in both directions (proceed OR kill), not just "review and continue."
79	6. **Pre-Human-Review Preparer and auto-merge mechanism** — `gh pr merge --auto` (GitHub native) vs Graphite's merge queue (`gt submit --merge-when-ready`-style). Default to Graphite-native since the rest of the PR workflow is Graphite.
80

Roles 2, 3, 6 already aligned.\n\n**Operator still in the loop at three points:** cycle planning (role 0), confirming grade templates (`draft` → `confirmed`), and reviewing PRs that route down the human-review path.\n\n## KR reshape\n\nThe override-down-to-2 from round 3 is no longer needed — the broader scope has three real, non-overlapping failure modes that each deserve a KR. Proposed new KR set (final wording in the redraft):\n\n- **KR1 — merge-clean rate on hard tickets** *(stretch, bet)*. Unchanged in spirit. ≥ 3 of next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits.\n- **KR2 — zero silent Dones** *(commit, brake)*. Zero exceptions.\n- **KR3 — every PR submitted under the new flow has the What / Why / Focus body, or is correctly routed to auto-merge** *(commit, brake)*. Default to Graphite-native since the rest of the PR workflow is Graphite.\n\n## Doc sections — what changes, what stays\n\n| Section | Change |\n|---|---|\n| §1 Raw idea | **Rewrite.** Lead with the six-role roster; webhook clarification summary; one-initiative scope statement. |\n| §2 Problem-or-solution gate | **Minor edit.** Still passes; update language so \"input refinement\" reads as \"Pre-Implementation Reviewer\" and add the PR-side failure mode. |\n| §3 Project type | **No change.** Still Type 2. |\n| §4 Negative outcome | **Extend.** Add the PR-side failure modes: PRs land without focus guidance, human review comments go unaddressed until operator manually re-invokes. |\n| §5 Affected repos | **Minor edit.** Note that `agent-skills-shaper` may receive one new skill (Outcome Verifier) if M2's open decision concludes that route. Otherwise drain-cycle remains the only repo touched. |\n| §6 Goal + KRs | **Rewrite.** New Goal sentence covering both worker-internal and PR-side surfaces. Three KRs (KR1 ship-clean, KR2 zero silent Dones, KR3 PR body structure or auto-merge). Keep the existing 5-tickets-across-2-cycles whole-initiative kill as the M2 gate; extend with M3/M4-specific kill cues. |\n| §9 Out of scope | **Rewrite.** Per § \"§9 net change\" above. |\n| §10 Open review points | **Reset.** Round 1/2/3 marked resolved; new round-4 list per \"open decisions\" above. |\n| §11 Next steps | **No change.** |\n\n## Verification (how the operator validates the redraft)\n\n1. After ExitPlanMode + redraft, the operator re-opens `crit docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md` (round 4) and leaves inline comments on any of §1, §6, §7, §9, §10 they want adjusted further.\n2. Two grading sub-checks at gate time:\n   - Roster table in §1 matches the roster table in this plan (six rows, three new).\n   - The §7 milestones each have a written gate condition.\n3. Once round 4 is approved with zero new comments, the redraft moves to Step 6.5 of `initiative-shape` — the 11-rule rubric. Rule 4 should now pass on its own (3 KRs), so no override is needed.\n\n## Out of scope for this redraft pass\n\n- Authoring the Linear project — that happens at Step 7 after rubric passes.\n- Writing the design doc (M1 slice 2 in old §7 numbering) — that's the next initiative phase, not this redraft.\n- Implementing any of M1–M5 — execution-time work, not draft-time.\n\n---\n\n## Skill inventory & UNIX-philosophy audit\n\nAudit of which skills run at each phase, split by invoker, to check the design decision 10 toolkit-symlink premise still holds (every skill callable from both contexts).\n\nLegend: `[E]` exists today · `[R]` exists, refined for narrower focus · `[N]` new, must be authored · `[—]` not a skill (CLI / built-in / scaffolding).\n\n### The framing this audit checks against\n\nPer operator review (c_b76ab7), the working split is:\n\n- **Human** drives the **why** (initiative goal + KRs) and the **what** (the outcome-focused Linear issue — agent-assisted, but human-led).\n- **Agents** handle the **how** — implementation, verification, PR preparation, feedback response — via a specific set of skills/workflows the human defines.\n- The **how** can be invoked manually (operator at the keyboard) OR automatically (drain-cycle running the same skills in a worker session). The skills are the same primitives in both cases.\n\nThe audit below splits skills by *invocation context* (operator vs worker), and within the worker context shows how each skill maps to a `how`-step the human predefined.\n\n### Skills *you* invoke during operator-side planning & manual work\n\n| Phase | Skill | Status | What it does |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Initiative shape | `/shape:initiative` | [E] | Six-field initiative gate + 11-rule rubric. |\n| Idea triage | `/idea-triage` | [E] | Frames new problems before they become tickets. |\n| Plan review | `/shape:plan-review` | [E] | Evaluates an existing plan against a rubric. |\n| Task breakdown | `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` | [E] | Decomposes a ticket/spec into sub-tasks. |\n| Spec writing | `/spec-driven-development` | [E] | Requirements + ACs before code. |\n| Design doc | `/shape:design-doc` | [E] | Larger-than-ticket architecture artefact. |\n| Doc / PR review | `/crit:crit` | [E] | Inline review of docs, plans, branch diffs, live URLs. |\n| ADR | `/documentation-and-adrs` | [E] | Captures architectural decisions. |\n| Code review (manual) | `/code-review-and-quality` | [E] | Five-axis review of branch/PR diff. |\n| Debugging | `/debugging-and-error-recovery` | [E] | Reproduce → localize → fix → guard. |\n| Implementation (manual) | `/incremental-implementation`, `/test-driven-development`, `/source-driven-development`, `/git-workflow-and-versioning`, `/shipping-and-launch` | [E] | Standard build/ship chain when you do the work yourself. |\n| Ticket assembly for drain queue | `/spec-driven-development` + `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` | [E] | Two-skill combo that produces a drain-ready ticket. |\n\n### Skills the *drain-cycle worker* invokes per ticket\n\n| Role / Phase | Skill | Status | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **1. Pre-Implementation Reviewer** — sizing + AC sufficiency + breakdown | `/shape:outcome-tasks` *(refined `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`)* | **[R]** | Refines the existing task-breakdown skill to focus on one outcome-focused task (typically a Linear issue). Inputs: the issue body + its ACs. Outputs: enriched AC checklist, sizing/stack-shape decision (1 stack vs N), per-stack implementation task list. Pairs symmetrically with `/shape:outcome-verify` (row 3). `/shape:plan-review` is the wrong fit — it operates on whole design docs, not single outcome-focused tasks (c_b76ab7). |\n| **2a. Implementer** — code-writing pass | `/incremental-implementation` | [E] | Worker's main loop. |\n| **2b. Implementer** — proof of behaviour | `/test-driven-development` | [E] | Runs alongside 2a. |\n| **2c. Implementer** — doc-verified API use | `/source-driven-development` | [E] | When touching external libs. |\n| **2d. Implementer** — code-review sub-agent (in-session) | `/code-review-and-quality` | [E] | Already mandated by `linear-workflow.md` as pre-Done step in worker. |\n| **2e. Implementer** — commits | `/git-workflow-and-versioning` | [E] | Stack-aware via Graphite MCP. |\n| **2f. Implementer** — error recovery | `/debugging-and-error-recovery` | [E] | When tests fail mid-session. |\n| **3. Outcome Verifier** — spec-adherence / outcome | new skill — working name `/shape:outcome-verify` | **[N]** | Genuinely new: takes ticket body + diff + run-log, asks \"did this build what was specified and did the outcome land,\" writes structured verdict to run log. Distinct from `/code-review-and-quality` in input (spec + diff, not just diff) and output (verdict for run-log, not five-axis review). Pairs symmetrically with `/shape:outcome-tasks` (row 1) — one defines the outcome-shaped task list, the other verifies the outcome was delivered. |\n| **4. Pre-Human-Review Preparer** — PR body + auto-merge route | new skill — working name `/shape:pr-prepare` | **[N]** | Reads stack PRs from Graphite MCP; writes What / Why / Focus body; tags auto-merge eligible vs human-review-needed. |\n| **5. PR-Feedback Responder** — address review comments | new skill — working name `/shape:pr-respond` | **[N]** | Reads PR review threads via Graphite MCP, makes the fix in the right worktree, pushes to stack. Trigger is drain-cycle-side (polling / Action / one-keystroke), but the skill itself is the same primitive regardless of trigger. |\n| **6. Grader** (auto-draft) | `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` CLI subcommand | **[—]** | Per operator review (c_832b0f): this is a CLI command on drain-cycle, not a skill. One-shot internal prompt: ticket + run-log + diff → fills the grade template with `status: draft`. Operator can invoke it manually on past tickets to retro-fill grades. |\n\n### Non-skill primitives the worker still uses\n\nIncluded so the audit is complete — these aren't skills but are the rest of the composition surface.\n\n| Primitive | What it is | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*` | Linear MCP — read/write issues, comments, status, labels, cycles | The work-queue interface. |\n| `mcp__graphite__*` | Graphite MCP — manage stacked PRs | The PR-side interface. |\n| `mcp__honeycomb__*` | Honeycomb MCP — observability | Operability surface; OTel spans already ship since `3eae2f5`. |\n| `drain-cycle` CLI | Dispatcher: worktree creation, worker spawning, run-log writing, grader invocation | Orchestration, not a skill. |\n| `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` | New subcommand — drafts the per-ticket grade template after a drain finishes (or retro on a past issue) | Per c_832b0f, replaces what was previously sketched as `/shape:grade-draft`. |\n| `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` | KR-grading subcommand — reads confirmed grade templates, reports pass-rate, warns about un-confirmed drafts | Carried over from round-3 doc. |\n| `gh` / `gt` CLIs | GitHub + Graphite shells | Called from inside skills (e.g. `/git-workflow-and-versioning`). |\n\n### UNIX-philosophy audit — findings\n\n**Strengths:**\n\n1. **Shared toolkit confirmed.** Every skill in the worker table is also invokable by you manually — decision 10's symlink working as intended. The four `[N]` skills inherit this property because they'll be authored as standard skills, not drain-cycle-internal logic.\n2. **Each skill is single-purpose.** No skill in either table does two unrelated things; the role-to-skill mapping is roughly 1:1, and where it's many-to-one (e.g. Implementer using five skills sequentially) it's because the *role* spans multiple phases, not because a skill is overloaded.\n3. **Reuse over new authoring where defensible.** Five of the six worker roles use existing skills; only three slots need genuinely new skills (plus one borderline).\n\n**Warnings:**\n\n1. **`/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` refinement scope.** Refining the existing skill to focus on a single outcome-focused task is conceptually clean (c_b76ab7), but the existing skill is general-purpose; the refinement may need either (a) a narrower variant alongside the general one, or (b) a full reshape with the general behaviour deprecated. Decide at M2 design time. Rename to `/shape:outcome-tasks` is the operator's preference (c_a406e2).\n2. **Three new skills is a lot for one initiative.** `/shape:outcome-verify`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, `/shape:pr-respond`. Each is justified individually, but it does push against universal Rule B6 (\"Generalise on the third occurrence, not the first\"). The mitigation: each skill has at least two invocation paths (operator-manual + worker-automated), so the \"third occurrence\" rule is effectively pre-satisfied. ## Approved Plan (edited by user):
# Plan — redraft `docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md` (round 4 reshape)

## Context

The initiative as currently drafted (rounds 1–3) covers two worker-internal roles: a *planner* (pre-implementation) and a *verifier* (pre-Done). PR-side roles were explicitly out of scope. In conversation today, the operator clarified:

- The "planner" framing was wrong — they already do that planning themselves; what they actually want before implementation is a **Pre-Implementation Reviewer** that decides *sizing/stack-shape* and checks *AC sufficiency*, not one that does the planning.
- The "verifier" overlaps with code-review (which already happens via a sub-agent during implementation). The Outcome Verifier is a *different* check: did the implementer build what the spec said, and were the stated outcomes reached?
- Two more roles belong in this initiative: a **Pre-Human-Review Preparer** that ensures every PR has What / Why / Focus structure (and routes to auto-merge if nothing critical), and a **PR-Feedback Responder** that addresses human review comments on Graphite PRs autonomously.
- One initiative, not two — but with explicit milestones so the bigger scope is tractable.

This plan describes the redraft for `docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md` that incorporates the reshape, then sends it back through crit (round 4).

## Final roster (the redrafted doc will list this in §1 / §5)

Seven roles total. Role 0 is the human; roles 1–6 are agent-driven. Each role maps 1:1 to exactly one entry in the skill inventory below — the `Skill / CLI` column makes that mapping explicit.

| # | Role | Phase | What it does | Skill / CLI (see inventory) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | **Operator** (you) | Before & throughout | Defines the *why* (initiative goal/KRs) and the *what* (outcome-focused Linear issue, agent-assisted but human-led) | Operator-side skill table (`/shape:initiative`, `/spec-driven-development`, `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` for ticket assembly, `/crit:crit`, etc.) | — |
| 1 | **Task Shaper** *(was: Pre-Implementation Reviewer)* | Before code | Refines the outcome-focused task into enriched ACs + sizing/stack-shape decision (1 stack vs N) + per-stack implementation task list. Carve-out on routing: even small work goes down human-review path if it requires human review AND won't block queue. | `/shape:outcome-tasks` (refined `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`) | **[R]** |
| 2 | **Implementer** | Middle | Worker session: build/test/source-verify/in-session-code-review/debug/commit chain. The standard "the worker writing code" phase. | 6 existing skills (`/incremental-implementation`, `/test-driven-development`, `/source-driven-development`, `/code-review-and-quality`, `/git-workflow-and-versioning`, `/debugging-and-error-recovery`) | [E] |
| 3 | **Outcome Verifier** | After implementation, before Done | Strictly: did the implementer build what the spec described, and were the outcomes reached? Not code-review (that ran in role 2). | `/shape:outcome-verify` | **[N]** |
| 4 | **PR Preparer** *(was: Pre-Human-Review Preparer)* | After Outcome Verifier passes, before PR submission | Walks each PR (or Graphite stack PR), writes the What / Why / What-reviewer-should-focus-on body. Routes to auto-merge if no critical focus items. | `/shape:pr-prepare` | **[N]** |
| 5 | **PR Responder** *(was: PR-Feedback Responder)* | Post-submission, on new human review comments | Reads new review comments, makes the fix in the right worktree, pushes to the stack. Trigger model has three plausible variants (see open decisions). | `/shape:pr-respond` | **[N]** |
| 6 | **Grader** (post-drain) | After worker session finishes | Drafts the per-ticket grade template at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` with `status: draft`. Operator confirms (`draft` → `confirmed`). | `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` CLI subcommand | [—] |

**Mapping check.** Six agent-driven roles (1–6); six entries in the worker-side skill inventory below. 1:1. Role 6 is the one outlier — it's CLI-driven rather than skill-driven (per c_832b0f), so the `Skill / CLI` column shows the CLI subcommand instead of a `/shape:` path.

**Naming alignment.** Roles 1, 4, 5 were renamed to mirror their skill names (`outcome-tasks` → Task Shaper, `pr-prepare` → PR Preparer, `pr-respond` → PR Responder). Roles 2, 3, 6 already aligned.

**Operator still in the loop at three points:** cycle planning (role 0), confirming grade templates (`draft` → `confirmed`), and reviewing PRs that route down the human-review path.

## KR reshape

The override-down-to-2 from round 3 is no longer needed — the broader scope has three real, non-overlapping failure modes that each deserve a KR. Proposed new KR set (final wording in the redraft):

- **KR1 — merge-clean rate on hard tickets** *(stretch, bet)*. Unchanged in spirit. ≥ 3 of next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits.
- **KR2 — zero silent Dones** *(commit, brake)*. Every Done in Linear under the new flow has an Outcome Verifier verdict in the run log. Zero exceptions.
- **KR3 — every PR submitted under the new flow has the What / Why / Focus body, or is correctly routed to auto-merge** *(commit, brake)*. PR Preparer either produced the structured body, or marked the PR auto-mergeable.

Rule 4 (≥ 3 KRs) now passes naturally; the round-3 override note in §6.3 is deleted.

The PR Responder doesn't get its own KR in v1 — its existence is structurally captured by KR1 (clean-merge rate would degrade if the responder doesn't address review feedback) and the Layer 1/2 dimensions stay balanced (correctness / maintenance / discipline) without a fourth KR.

## Appetite & milestone restructure (this is the big change to §7)

Old §7 was a flat list of 12 slices. New §7 reorganises around five milestones. Each milestone has a stop-the-line gate: if the milestone's gate fails, the initiative pauses for a kill/reshape decision before the next milestone starts. The total slice count grows from ~12 to ~18–22.

- **M1 — Recording infrastructure.** `verify` label parser; run-log schema fields (`flow`, `outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, optional `responder_runs[]`); matching OTel attributes on `drain.issue`; `docs/design-decisions.md` §1 supersession. Gate: an end-to-end "verify"-labelled ticket drains, records the new fields, no behaviour change yet.
- **M2 — Worker-internal loop.** Task Shaper (`/shape:outcome-tasks` refined), Implementer integration (no change beyond contract), Outcome Verifier (`/shape:outcome-verify` — new skill; `/code-review-and-quality` is *not* the right fit here), halt-on-fail path. Gate: 3 verify-labelled hard tickets drained; verdicts recorded; halt path exercised at least once on a deliberate fail.
- **M3 — PR-shape gate.** PR Preparer (`/shape:pr-prepare`); structured PR body writer; auto-merge routing logic; integration with Graphite stack output. Gate: 3 stacks emitted, all with What / Why / Focus bodies or auto-merge labels.
- **M4 — PR-feedback loop.** PR Responder (`/shape:pr-respond`); trigger mechanism (defaulting to polling at drain-start, per Q2 conversation — open decision below); fix-push to stack; idempotency / dedup of already-addressed comments. Gate: one real PR with operator feedback gets addressed by the responder end-to-end.
- **M5 — Grading + cost + close-out.** Per-ticket grade template (with auto-draft from round 2); `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify`; silent-Done detection; cost-impact handling in `limits.yml`; post-launch review against KRs. Gate: KR grading runs; one of {ship cleanly, pause, kill} decided.

The plan file the worker eventually breaks this into (via `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`) will name the individual slices per milestone.

## §9 (out of scope) — net change

**Moves into scope:** PR Responder / feedback loop (M4); PR Preparer (M3).

**Stays out of scope:**
- Parallel drains (still orthogonal; decision §8 unchanged).
- Authoring new skills in `agent-skills-shaper` — *except* if M2's open decision below concludes the Outcome Verifier needs a new bespoke skill, in which case that one is allowed.

**New out-of-scope items to add:**
- Multi-operator / multi-tenant workflows (single-user product per §3).
- Replacing Graphite itself (the PR-feedback work assumes the existing Graphite stack flow).

## Open decisions to flag in new §10 (round 4)

These are the genuinely open ones the operator hasn't pinned yet. Each will be a numbered point in §10:

1. **Outcome Verifier as a new skill** — confirmed in the audit as `[N]` (`/shape:outcome-verify`); flagged here for §10 visibility because it pairs symmetrically with `/shape:outcome-tasks`. No alternative on the table.
2. **PR Responder trigger model** — three plausible options (polling at drain-start / GitHub Action routing to Linear ticket / one-keystroke `drain-cycle respond <pr>`). Default in the redraft: *polling at drain-start*, per operator's daemon-aversion. Webhook clarification (no new subscription needed; constraint is *where the agent runs*) summarised in §1.
3. **Sizing rule threshold** — per Q3, default to agent-judged + reasoning logged. Confirm there's no fallback numeric threshold (e.g. ≥ N files) wanted as a guardrail.
4. **KR3 commit-vs-stretch** — currently proposed as commit/brake. Confirm or flip to stretch.
5. **Milestone gates as kill points** — confirm gates are stop-the-line in both directions (proceed OR kill), not just "review and continue."
6. **PR Preparer and auto-merge mechanism** — `gh pr merge --auto` (GitHub native) vs Graphite's merge queue (`gt submit --merge-when-ready`-style). Default to Graphite-native since the rest of the PR workflow is Graphite.

## Doc sections — what changes, what stays

| Section | Change |
|---|---|
| §1 Raw idea | **Rewrite.** Lead with the six-role roster; webhook clarification summary; one-initiative scope statement. |
| §2 Problem-or-solution gate | **Minor edit.** Still passes; update language so "input refinement" reads as "Pre-Implementation Reviewer" and add the PR-side failure mode. |
| §3 Project type | **No change.** Still Type 2. |
| §4 Negative outcome | **Extend.** Add the PR-side failure modes: PRs land without focus guidance, human review comments go unaddressed until operator manually re-invokes. |
| §5 Affected repos | **Minor edit.** Note that `agent-skills-shaper` may receive one new skill (Outcome Verifier) if M2's open decision concludes that route. Otherwise drain-cycle remains the only repo touched. |
| §6 Goal + KRs | **Rewrite.** New Goal sentence covering both worker-internal and PR-side surfaces. Three KRs (KR1 ship-clean, KR2 zero silent Dones, KR3 PR body structure or auto-merge). Drop the round-3 override on rule 4. |
| §7 Appetite | **Rewrite.** Move from flat 12-slice list to five-milestone structure (M1–M5) with gates. Total grows to ~18–22 issues. |
| §8 Kill condition | **Edit.** Add per-milestone gate behaviour. Keep the existing 5-tickets-across-2-cycles whole-initiative kill as the M2 gate; extend with M3/M4-specific kill cues. |
| §9 Out of scope | **Rewrite.** Per § "§9 net change" above. |
| §10 Open review points | **Reset.** Round 1/2/3 marked resolved; new round-4 list per "open decisions" above. |
| §11 Next steps | **No change.** |

## Verification (how the operator validates the redraft)

1. After ExitPlanMode + redraft, the operator re-opens `crit docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md` (round 4) and leaves inline comments on any of §1, §6, §7, §9, §10 they want adjusted further.
2. Two grading sub-checks at gate time:
   - Roster table in §1 matches the roster table in this plan (six rows, three new).
   - The §7 milestones each have a written gate condition.
3. Once round 4 is approved with zero new comments, the redraft moves to Step 6.5 of `initiative-shape` — the 11-rule rubric. Rule 4 should now pass on its own (3 KRs), so no override is needed.

## Out of scope for this redraft pass

- Authoring the Linear project — that happens at Step 7 after rubric passes.
- Writing the design doc (M1 slice 2 in old §7 numbering) — that's the next initiative phase, not this redraft.
- Implementing any of M1–M5 — execution-time work, not draft-time.

## Skill inventory & UNIX-philosophy audit

Audit of which skills run at each phase, split by invoker, to check the design decision 10 toolkit-symlink premise still holds (every skill callable from both contexts).

Legend: `[E]` exists today · `[R]` exists, refined for narrower focus · `[N]` new, must be authored · `[—]` not a skill (CLI / built-in / scaffolding).

### The framing this audit checks against

Per operator review (c_b76ab7), the working split is:

- **Human** drives the **why** (initiative goal + KRs) and the **what** (the outcome-focused Linear issue — agent-assisted, but human-led).
- **Agents** handle the **how** — implementation, verification, PR preparation, feedback response — via a specific set of skills/workflows the human defines.
- The **how** can be invoked manually (operator at the keyboard) OR automatically (drain-cycle running the same skills in a worker session). The skills are the same primitives in both cases.

The audit below splits skills by *invocation context* (operator vs worker), and within the worker context shows how each skill maps to a `how`-step the human predefined.

### Skills *you* invoke during operator-side planning & manual work

| Phase | Skill | Status | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Initiative shape | `/shape:initiative` | [E] | Six-field initiative gate + 11-rule rubric. |
| Idea triage | `/idea-triage` | [E] | Frames new problems before they become tickets. |
| Plan review | `/shape:plan-review` | [E] | Evaluates an existing plan against a rubric. |
| Task breakdown | `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` | [E] | Decomposes a ticket/spec into sub-tasks. |
| Spec writing | `/spec-driven-development` | [E] | Requirements + ACs before code. |
| Design doc | `/shape:design-doc` | [E] | Larger-than-ticket architecture artefact. |
| Doc / PR review | `/crit:crit` | [E] | Inline review of docs, plans, branch diffs, live URLs. |
| ADR | `/documentation-and-adrs` | [E] | Captures architectural decisions. |
| Code review (manual) | `/code-review-and-quality` | [E] | Five-axis review of branch/PR diff. |
| Debugging | `/debugging-and-error-recovery` | [E] | Reproduce → localize → fix → guard. |
| Implementation (manual) | `/incremental-implementation`, `/test-driven-development`, `/source-driven-development`, `/git-workflow-and-versioning`, `/shipping-and-launch` | [E] | Standard build/ship chain when you do the work yourself. |
| Ticket assembly for drain queue | `/spec-driven-development` + `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` | [E] | Two-skill combo that produces a drain-ready ticket. |

### Skills the *drain-cycle worker* invokes per ticket

| Role / Phase | Skill | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1. Pre-Implementation Reviewer** — sizing + AC sufficiency + breakdown | `/shape:outcome-tasks` *(refined `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`)* | **[R]** | Refines the existing task-breakdown skill to focus on one outcome-focused task (typically a Linear issue). Inputs: the issue body + its ACs. Outputs: enriched AC checklist, sizing/stack-shape decision (1 stack vs N), per-stack implementation task list. Pairs symmetrically with `/shape:outcome-verify` (row 3). `/shape:plan-review` is the wrong fit — it operates on whole design docs, not single outcome-focused tasks (c_b76ab7). |
| **2a. Implementer** — code-writing pass | `/incremental-implementation` | [E] | Worker's main loop. |
| **2b. Implementer** — proof of behaviour | `/test-driven-development` | [E] | Runs alongside 2a. |
| **2c. Implementer** — doc-verified API use | `/source-driven-development` | [E] | When touching external libs. |
| **2d. Implementer** — code-review sub-agent (in-session) | `/code-review-and-quality` | [E] | Already mandated by `linear-workflow.md` as pre-Done step in worker. |
| **2e. Implementer** — commits | `/git-workflow-and-versioning` | [E] | Stack-aware via Graphite MCP. |
| **2f. Implementer** — error recovery | `/debugging-and-error-recovery` | [E] | When tests fail mid-session. |
| **3. Outcome Verifier** — spec-adherence / outcome | new skill — working name `/shape:outcome-verify` | **[N]** | Genuinely new: takes ticket body + diff + run-log, asks "did this build what was specified and did the outcome land," writes structured verdict to run log. Distinct from `/code-review-and-quality` in input (spec + diff, not just diff) and output (verdict for run-log, not five-axis review). Pairs symmetrically with `/shape:outcome-tasks` (row 1) — one defines the outcome-shaped task list, the other verifies the outcome was delivered. |
| **4. Pre-Human-Review Preparer** — PR body + auto-merge route | new skill — working name `/shape:pr-prepare` | **[N]** | Reads stack PRs from Graphite MCP; writes What / Why / Focus body; tags auto-merge eligible vs human-review-needed. |
| **5. PR-Feedback Responder** — address review comments | new skill — working name `/shape:pr-respond` | **[N]** | Reads PR review threads via Graphite MCP, makes the fix in the right worktree, pushes to stack. Trigger is drain-cycle-side (polling / Action / one-keystroke), but the skill itself is the same primitive regardless of trigger. |
| **6. Grader** (auto-draft) | `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` CLI subcommand | **[—]** | Per operator review (c_832b0f): this is a CLI command on drain-cycle, not a skill. One-shot internal prompt: ticket + run-log + diff → fills the grade template with `status: draft`. Operator can invoke it manually on past tickets to retro-fill grades. |

### Non-skill primitives the worker still uses

Included so the audit is complete — these aren't skills but are the rest of the composition surface.

| Primitive | What it is | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__*` | Linear MCP — read/write issues, comments, status, labels, cycles | The work-queue interface. |
| `mcp__graphite__*` | Graphite MCP — manage stacked PRs | The PR-side interface. |
| `mcp__honeycomb__*` | Honeycomb MCP — observability | Operability surface; OTel spans already ship since `3eae2f5`. |
| `drain-cycle` CLI | Dispatcher: worktree creation, worker spawning, run-log writing, grader invocation | Orchestration, not a skill. |
| `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` | New subcommand — drafts the per-ticket grade template after a drain finishes (or retro on a past issue) | Per c_832b0f, replaces what was previously sketched as `/shape:grade-draft`. |
| `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` | KR-grading subcommand — reads confirmed grade templates, reports pass-rate, warns about un-confirmed drafts | Carried over from round-3 doc. |
| `gh` / `gt` CLIs | GitHub + Graphite shells | Called from inside skills (e.g. `/git-workflow-and-versioning`). |

### UNIX-philosophy audit — findings

**Strengths:**

1. **Shared toolkit confirmed.** Every skill in the worker table is also invokable by you manually — decision 10's symlink working as intended. The four `[N]` skills inherit this property because they'll be authored as standard skills, not drain-cycle-internal logic.
2. **Each skill is single-purpose.** No skill in either table does two unrelated things; the role-to-skill mapping is roughly 1:1, and where it's many-to-one (e.g. Implementer using five skills sequentially) it's because the *role* spans multiple phases, not because a skill is overloaded.
3. **Reuse over new authoring where defensible.** Five of the six worker roles use existing skills; only three slots need genuinely new skills (plus one borderline).

**Warnings:**

1. **`/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` refinement scope.** Refining the existing skill to focus on a single outcome-focused task is conceptually clean (c_b76ab7), but the existing skill is general-purpose; the refinement may need either (a) a narrower variant alongside the general one, or (b) a full reshape with the general behaviour deprecated. Decide at M2 design time. Rename to `/shape:outcome-tasks` is the operator's preference (c_a406e2).
2. **Three new skills is a lot for one initiative.** `/shape:outcome-verify`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, `/shape:pr-respond`. Each is justified individually, but it does push against universal Rule B6 ("Generalise on the third occurrence, not the first"). The mitigation: each skill has at least two invocation paths (operator-manual + worker-automated), so the "third occurrence" rule is effectively pre-satisfied. Still worth being explicit about in the design doc.
3. **No formal landing for the sizing decision's *output*.** `/shape:outcome-tasks` decides "split into 3 stacks." That decision needs to land somewhere actionable — worktree file, Linear sub-tickets, or inline implementer prompt? Currently undefined; flag for M2 design doc.

**Counts:**

- Existing skills reused as-is: 9
- Existing skills refined for narrower focus: 1 (`/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` → `/shape:outcome-tasks`)
- New skills authored: 3 (`/shape:outcome-verify`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, `/shape:pr-respond`)
- New drain-cycle CLI subcommands: 2 (`drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>`, `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify`)
- New non-skill scaffolding (`verify` label parser, run-log fields, OTel attrs, grading template): drain-cycle-internal.

### Open audit decisions (to fold into §10 of the redraft alongside existing open decisions)

A. **`/shape:outcome-tasks` rename adoption** — confirm renaming the existing `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` skill to `/shape:outcome-tasks` for symmetric pairing with `/shape:outcome-verify`. Cost: cross-references in other repos and `agent-skills-shaper` may need updating. Default: adopt the rename.

B. **Refinement style for `/shape:outcome-tasks`** — full reshape of the existing skill (deprecating the general-purpose behaviour), or a narrower variant alongside it? Flag for M2 design doc.

C. **Three-new-skill budget** — confirm comfort with authoring three new skills (`/shape:outcome-verify`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, `/shape:pr-respond`) as part of this initiative, or push some into a sibling skill-authoring initiative.

D. **Where the sizing decision lands** — worktree file? Linear sub-tickets? Inline implementer prompt? Flag for M2 design doc; doesn't need answering now.
