> AGENT

Good — that's the third surface, and it's the one that's currently unautomated. Naming it explicitly:

- **Input side**: ticket refinement → you have `crit` + your own review ✓
- **Output (pre-merge)**: planner/verifier loop → discussed last turn, doesn't exist yet
- **Output (post-merge-request, post-human-review)**: PR comment → agent picks up → amends → re-pushes → optionally replies → human re-reviews → ... ✗ this is the gap

You already have a human-in-the-loop pattern that works (`crit` for plans). The missing piece is the same pattern on the code side, with Graphite as the review surface rather than crit.

## The constraint forces the architecture

Your subscription-only constraint rules out the obvious path (the Anthropic `claude-code-action` GitHub Action runs on API keys in CI). That means the loop runs **locally**, on the machine where `claude` is logged in. No GitHub-side automation. Whatever picks up the comments has to be a process you own.

That collapses the trigger question into three honest options — and you should pick deliberately, because each has a different footprint:

- **Polling daemon** (gastown's `gt patrol` shape) — periodic `gh pr view` across your active stack, dispatches on new comments. Fully automatic; you've explicitly avoided this shape in drain-cycle, and it has real failure modes (rate limits, runaway loops, "agent addresses comment you were still writing").
- **One-keystroke command** — `drain-cycle feedback` (or sibling tool) lists PRs with unaddressed comments, you pick one or all, it runs. Not "without kicking off" in the absolute sense, but it's one command per review cycle, not per comment. Matches drain-cycle's existing manual-but-unattended ergonomics.
- **Hook-driven** — git `post-fetch` hook, or a local webhook receiver via tailscale/ngrok. More setup, but it fires on signal rather than schedule.

The honest framing: "without manually kicking it off" is a UX goal, not an implementation; you'll trade some automation for some control, and the right trade depends on how often you review.

## What "address the comment" actually means as a contract

This is where the design is non-trivial. The agent's per-comment output should be one of:

- **addressed-by-commit `<sha>`** — produced a code change, points at the commit
- **replied-with-reasoning** — answered in the thread, no code change (e.g. "this is intentional because …")
- **pushed back** — disagrees with the comment, says so, awaits your call (per your principle: not a yes-machine)
- **deferred** — out of scope for this PR, surfaced as a new ticket

Plus a per-PR summary. Resolving threads stays your decision, not the agent's — that's the human gate that keeps the loop honest.

## This is now bigger than drain-cycle

drain-cycle's contract is "ticket → first push, then exit." The feedback loop is "from-push-onward, until merged." That's a different lifecycle stage, a different trigger, and a different blast radius (amending pushed code vs writing new code). The clean shape is **a sibling tool**, sharing primitives (worktree management, `claude -p` orchestration, guardrails, run-log, OTel) but with its own command. Don't grow drain-cycle to cover it — the existing decisions (one cycle per invocation, agent self-asserts Done) don't extend cleanly. Worth naming the suite at some point; not yet.

## Smallest validatable slice for this surface

A manual experiment, in your existing flow:

1. Take one stacked PR you've already reviewed (real comments you left).
2. Hand-construct the input: `gh pr view <n> --json comments,reviews,headRefName,baseRefName` + `gh pr diff <n>`.
3. Run `claude -p` in the relevant worktree with a prompt that names the contract above ("for each comment, produce: addressed/replied/pushed-back/deferred + reasoning + commit if applicable, do not resolve threads").
4. Read the output. Did it address what you actually meant? Did it push back where it should have? Did it touch things you didn't ask about?

If the output is good on one real PR, the loop is shippable as a one-keystroke command. If it isn't — you've learned whether the issue is context-loading, prompt shape, or comment-as-signal noise, before you build any trigger.

> AGENT

That's the cleanest frame yet — and it reorganises a lot of what I just said.

## The unifying philosophy

drain-cycle isn't an orchestration system that builds bespoke pipelines. It's a **batch/unattended driver for an ecosystem of small composable primitives (skills + MCPs)**, with two interfaces to the same primitives:

- **Manual mode** — you open Claude Code, you invoke `/crit`, `/shape:plan-review`, `/code-review-and-quality`, your MCPs, whatever — *you* choose the sequence
- **Ticket-driven mode** — you write the intent into a Linear ticket; drain-cycle dispatches an agent that invokes the same skills against the same primitives, unattended

Decision 10 (symlinking `.claude/` into worktrees) was already laying the groundwork for this without naming it: the worker has the exact same skill + MCP environment you do. The "fresh-context verifier" I was sketching as a custom subprocess is really just `/code-review-and-quality` invoked late in the worker's session — a primitive you already use manually. The planner is `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` invoked early. The pieces exist; what's missing is the contract that says *which skills must run, in what order, before Done*.

## What this changes about earlier suggestions

- **The verifier is no longer a separate subprocess.** It's a mandated skill invocation in the worker prompt. Much less plumbing.
- **The PR feedback loop may not need a sibling tool.** A `/address-pr-feedback` skill (or MCP) is manually invokable today; the same skill driven by a "address feedback on PR <n>" Linear ticket *is* ticket-driven mode. Two doors, one primitive. Sibling tool only earns its place if the trigger model needs to live outside Linear.
- **The genuinely new design surface is the contract**, not the orchestration: a per-ticket spec of "required skill sequence before Done." Plausibly Linear-label-driven, mirroring the `repo:` / `model:` mechanisms from decisions 5 and 7. A `flow:plan-implement-verify` label means the worker runs planner-skill → implementer → verifier-skill in that order, halting on verifier findings of given severity. Default flow for ops issues; richer flows opt-in.

## The mental model worth keeping

It's effectively UNIX-philosophy for agent work:

- **Skills + MCPs** = the small composable tools
- **Linear ticket** = the work-queue entry (the operator's intent as data)
- **Worker session** = the shell that runs the composition
- **drain-cycle** = cron/dispatch for the queue
- **Manual Claude Code** = interactive equivalent

Throughput is "easy" because the primitives are independent — you scale by adding skills/MCPs, not by parallelising workers. Correctness is the bet because the composition contract is where rigour lives.

I'll extend the memory to capture this — it's the load-bearing frame.

Design-doc — and this is actually one level up: it's **initiative-shaped**, not just design-doc-shaped. Three concrete tests from your own rules:

- **Universal Rule A1 triggers all fire**: more than one slice (three surfaces × multi-slice each), reuse (the contract pattern persists across every future drained ticket), meaningful impact (supersedes decision 1, changes worker behaviour, reframes the project's stated goal). One trigger is enough; you have all three.
- **`linear-workflow.md` says initiative**: this is a sustained, multi-cycle, goal-directed body of work with a measurable success criterion ("worker enforces a required-skill-sequence contract; one complex unsliceable ticket survives a drain with verifier-gated Done"). That's the OKR shape your own model demands — not a one-off ticket.
- **One-way door inside it**: rewriting the worker's Done contract (decision 1's supersession) is reversible in theory but expensive enough that "write the alternatives down" earns its keep. Per universal Principle 3, deliberation budget scales with reversibility cost.

## Recommended sequence

1. **`shape:initiative`** — Linear project with goal sentence + 3 KRs + appetite + kill condition + project type. Anchors WHY before HOW. This is the bet.
2. **`shape:design-doc`** — architecture: the unifying philosophy (skills as primitives, ticket-driven & manual modes), the three surfaces, the required-skill-sequence contract, decision-1 supersession, alternatives considered, NFRs (per-ticket cost multiplier, blast-radius of label-driven flows). First slice named as a *spike*, not a build.
3. **First slice = planner spike** (via `shape:backend-spike` or just a small experiment): "does a fresh-context planner produce useful AC checklists on three real tickets; does 'unsliceable' actually hold?" The result feeds back into the doc — confirms, reshapes, or kills the planner-as-slicer assumption.
4. **Subsequent slices**: lighter ADR per piece as they come up. Don't over-design what you'll validate later.

## What the doc shouldn't try to do

Lock all three surfaces down. That violates the "piece by piece as I validate" you stated, and universal Principle 7 (certainty decays with time horizon). The doc names the direction, the contract concept, the first slice, and explicitly defers the other surfaces as "approach options, decided per upcoming cycle when their slice comes up." Strong on the philosophy, deliberately sparse on Surfaces 2 and 3.

Want me to start with `shape:initiative` now, or do you want to think on the goal sentence and KRs first and come back to it?

## Step 1 — raw idea (verbatim)

> drain-cycle evolves from "drain a Linear cycle unattended" toward a **batch/unattended driver for an ecosystem of small composable primitives** (skills + MCPs), with Linear tickets as the work queue. The operator failure mode it addresses: **single complex tickets that can't be sliced further, where the implementing agent forgets details or under-verifies**. The bet is multi-agent collaboration across three surfaces — input refinement (pre-implementation ticket critique), output verification (fresh-context critic of the diff before Done), and PR feedback loop (autonomous response to human review on Graphite stacks). Skills + MCPs are composable primitives the worker invokes; ticket-driven and manual modes share the same toolkit. Subscription auth only (Claude Code / Codex CLI). Correctness > throughput.

## Step 2 [GATE] — problem or solution?

## Step 2.5 — project type

I'd classify this as **Type 2: personal product, single-user**.

- One user (you), so growth/MAU/retention KRs are categorically inapplicable — Type 2 playbook applies cleanly (correctness + no-silent-failure + maintenance-burden, optional availability 4th)

**Where this could plausibly be different:** if you see the *headline output* as a new skill pack (e.g. authoring `/plan-ticket`, `/verify-ticket`, `/address-pr-feedback` as a coherent pack in `agent-skills-shaper`, with drain-cycle as a secondary integrator), that's Type 1 (methodology) — and the KR mix changes materially to invocation-rate + decision-quality + anti-output guard. Type 2 confirmed. Loading the Type 2 playbook (correctness + no-silent-failure + maintenance-burden, with availability optional 4th — skipped, drain-cycle is operator-invoked, not scheduled).

One playbook substitution I'd propose for this specific initiative: **swap maintenance-burden for a discipline/foundation KR** in the third slot. Reason: the load-bearing addition here is instrumentation that doesn't exist yet (per-step skill verdicts in the run log + OTel trace) — without it the correctness KR isn't gradable post-hoc, which makes foundation more load-bearing than overhead-as-brake for this bet specifically. I'll flag both options below and let you pick.

## Step 3 — outcome questions, with proposed answers

I'll propose; mark up what's wrong.

### Who is affected
You (single operator). Type 2 default.

### Negative outcome if not solved
Complex Linear tickets that can't be sliced further get drained today, the worker self-asserts Done on exit, and the diff routinely misses stated AC or skips thoroughness checks. The current contract (decision 1) has no answer for "the agent thought it finished but missed half the requirements." You find out at PR review, manually re-invoke claude or fix by hand, and the unattended-drain promise breaks on exactly the tickets where it matters most.

### KRs — proposed

**KR1 (stretch)** — Verifier-gated correctness on complex tickets *(bet)*
- baseline: 0 (no verifier today; worker self-asserts Done)
- target: ≥ 80 % of `flow:plan-implement-verify` drains pass `/code-review-and-quality` on first verifier pass with no Critical/Required findings
- measured over: first 10 contract-enabled drains
- how we'll know: `drain-cycle grade <cycle-id>` extended to report verifier-verdict pass-rate per contract-enabled cycle (grader-backed; new field in run log)

*Layer 1: correctness · Layer 2: golden-path test*

**KR2 (commit)** — No silent Done transitions *(brake)*
- baseline: silent self-assertion currently possible (decision 1's contract)
- target: 0 silent Dones across all contract-enabled drains
- measured over: every contract-enabled cycle for the initiative duration
- how we'll know: `drain-cycle grade` fails any entry where `final_linear_state == "completed"` AND `skill_sequence_trace` is absent

*Layer 1: maintenance · Layer 2: structural cap + revisit gate*

**KR3 (commit)** — Skill-sequence trace instrumentation in place *(foundation)*
- baseline: skill-sequence trace doesn't exist in the run-log schema
- target: 100 % of contract-enabled entries carry the full trace (planner verdict, implementer claims, verifier verdict, halt reason if applicable) in both run log and OTel spans
- measured over: first 10 contract-enabled drains
- how we'll know: jq-based schema check against `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*.json`; CI test in drain-cycle runs the check on a recorded fixture

*Layer 1: discipline · Layer 2: artefact-exists + sections-complete*

*Dimensions: correctness / maintenance / discipline — all distinct per rule 5b.*

### Affected repos
- `drain-cycle` — primary (worker, run-log schema, `flow:` label mechanism, grade extensions, design-decisions §1 supersession)
- `agent-skills-shaper` — secondary if `flow:` becomes a documented convention or any new skill (e.g. `/verify-ticket`) is authored here rather than reusing existing ones

### Appetite
**~12 issues** (Medium-to-Large). Indicative slices (planning-and-task-breakdown comes later):
1. Planner spike on 3 real tickets
2. `shape:design-doc` for the required-skill-sequence contract
3. `flow:` label mechanism (mirrors `repo:` / `model:` from decisions 5/7)
4. Run-log schema + OTel attribute extensions
5–6. Worker integration: planner pre-step (invoke `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`)
7–8. Worker integration: verifier post-step (invoke `/code-review-and-quality`), Done-gate
9. Halt-on-verifier-fail + revert path (extends decision 8/9 machinery)
10. `drain-cycle grade` extensions for KR1/KR2/KR3 graders
11. Cost-impact tuning (per-flow multipliers in `limits.yml` if cycle cap pressure)
12. `docs/design-decisions.md` supersession of decision 1

### Kill condition
If, after running contract-enabled flows on **5+ complex tickets across 2 cycles**, the verifier produces **zero true-positive findings** (every halt was a false positive, and verifier-passed Dones don't visibly improve PR-review outcomes vs non-contract drains) — the planner+verifier overhead isn't earning its keep. Revert the contract; keep the instrumentation; record the negative result in `docs/design-decisions.md`.

### PR feedback loop — explicitly out of scope
Sibling initiative. Different trigger problem, different blast radius, shares only the skill-as-primitive philosophy.

33	Existing skills (`/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`, `/code-review-and-quality`) are reused as-is; no new skills authored in `agent-skills-shaper` as part of this initiative.
34	
35	## §6 Key results — draft for review
36	
37	Type 2 playbook default mix is **correctness + no-silent-failure + maintenance-burden**. KR3 is the slot under discussion (§6.4 below).
38	
39	### §6.1 KR1 (stretch) — Verifier-gated correctness survives a real complex ticket *(bet)*
40	
41	- **observable claim:** at least one complex unsliceable ticket bearing `flow:plan-implement-verify` completes a contract drain with verifier-gated Done and merges to main with no operator-required fix on PR review
42	- **baseline:** 0 (no verifier today; worker self-asserts Done per decision §1)
43	- **target:** ≥ 1 such ticket
44	- **measured over:** the initiative's lifetime (existence proof; grades on first hit)
45	- **how we'll know:** `drain-cycle grade <cycle-id>` extended to report `contract_pass_count` per cycle — counts entries where `flow_label=="plan-implement-verify"` AND `verifier_verdict=="pass"` AND `final_linear_state=="completed"`; manual confirmation that the corresponding PR merged with no operator-pushed fix commits
46	
47	*Layer 1: correctness · Layer 2: golden-path test*
48	
49	**Review notes:**
50	- Single-trial existence proof per user direction. Less ambitious than a rate-based KR but easier to grade cleanly and avoids gaming the threshold.
51	- "No operator-required fix on PR review" is the load-bearing modifier — without it, "completes a drain" is just "the agent didn't crash." Open question: is "no operator-required fix" verified manually each time, or do we want a stricter automated proxy (e.g. `gt log --behind=origin/main` showing zero post-drain commits to that branch from the operator)?
52	
53	### §6.2 KR2 (commit) — No silent Done transitions *(brake)*
54	
55	- **observable claim:** across all contract-enabled drains, zero issues reach Done in Linear without a matching verifier verdict and full skill-sequence trace in the run log
56	- **baseline:** silent self-assertion currently possible (decision §1's contract); count is unknown because the trace doesn't exist yet
57	- **target:** 0 silent Dones
58	- **measured over:** every contract-enabled cycle for the initiative's duration
59	- **how we'll know:** `drain-cycle grade` fails any entry where `final_linear_state=="completed"` AND (`skill_sequence_trace` absent OR `verifier_verdict` absent) — emits exit code ≠ 0 on any violation
60	
61	*Layer 1: maintenance · Layer 2: structural cap + revisit gate*
62	
63	**Review notes:**
64	- This is the property that supersedes decision §1: the worker's self-claim is no longer authoritative; the verifier verdict is.
65	- Implication for decision §1: needs explicit ADR-style supersession in `docs/design-decisions.md` (scoped as slice 12).
66	
67	### §6.3 KR3 — slot under discussion
68	
69	Two viable shapes. Pick A, B, or "both with a 4th KR" via feedback below.
70	
71	#### §6.3.A Option A — Instrumentation/foundation *(foundation, commit)*
72	
73	- **observable claim:** every contract-enabled drain emits a complete skill-sequence trace (planner verdict + AC checklist, implementer claims, verifier verdict, halt reason if applicable) in both the run log and the OTel trace
74	- **baseline:** skill-sequence trace doesn't exist in the run-log schema
75	- **target:** 100 % of contract-enabled entries carry the full trace
76	- **measured over:** first 10 contract-enabled drains
77	- **how we'll know:** jq-based schema check against `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*.json` fixture in CI; `drain-cycle grade` reports `trace_complete_rate`
78	
79	*Layer 1: discipline · Layer 2: artefact-exists + sections-complete*
80	
81	**Why this might be the right slot:** KR1 isn't gradable post-hoc without the instrumentation. The foundation KR is load-bearing for the bet KR; without it, the lagging correctness signal collapses into a vibe check.
82	
83	#### §6.3.B Option B — Maintenance-burden *(brake, commit)*
84	
85	- **observable claim:** the contract doesn't add measurable operator overhead per drained cycle — false-positive verifier halts and contract-related manual restarts stay below a tolerance
86	- **baseline:** 0 operator-touch events per cycle today (drain runs unattended)
87	- **target:** ≤ 2 operator-touch events per contract-enabled cycle (false-positive halts, manual restarts, contract-related re-runs)
88	- **measured over:** first 5 contract-enabled cycles
89	- **how we'll know:** new field in the run log (`operator_touch_events: [{kind, at, reason}]`) populated manually after each drain; `drain-cycle grade` sums them per cycle
90	
91	*Layer 1: maintenance · Layer 2: ???* (would conflict with KR2 on rule 5b — both maintenance dimension)
92	
93	**Why this might NOT work:** rule 5b ("KRs name distinct Layer 1 dimensions") blocks this — KR2 already claims the maintenance dimension. Option B is only viable if KR2's dimension is reclassified (e.g. as outcome) or if KR2 is dropped/replaced. Worth knowing before picking.
94	
95	#### §6.3.C Option C — Both (4 KRs total, with foundation as KR3 and maintenance as KR4)
96	
97	- KR1 (correctness, bet, stretch) — as §6.1
98	- KR2 (maintenance: no silent Done, brake, commit) — as §6.2
99	- KR3 (discipline: instrumentation, foundation, commit) — as §6.3.A
100	- KR4 (??? : maintenance: overhead, brake, commit) — as §6.3.B
101	
102	**Problem:** KR4 and KR2 still share Layer 1 dimension (both maintenance) and would fail rule 5b. To make Option C work, either reclassify one (e.g. KR2 as outcome — "the worker's Done claim is verified by an independent gate") or drop one of them. The skill caps KRs at 5 and warns against padding, so we should commit to a clean 3-KR mix unless a 4th dimension is genuinely load-bearing.
103	
104	#### §6.3 — Recommendation
105	
106	**Lean toward Option A (foundation).** Reasons:
107	1. KR1 isn't gradable without the trace — the foundation is structurally required.
108	2. KR2 already covers the no-silent-failure property that Type 2's playbook defaults to.
109	3. Maintenance-burden as a target can live in the **kill condition** (§8) rather than as a KR — "if the contract adds > 2 operator-touch events per cycle, kill it" — which is honest about it being a stopping condition rather than a thing we're tracking weekly.
110	
111	**Open question for review:** confirm A, or push back if you actually want B/C.
112	
113	## §7 Appetite
114	
115	**~12 issues — Medium-to-Large.**
116	
117	Indicative slice sketch (planning-and-task-breakdown produces the real list later; not a commitment to this exact decomposition):
118	
119	1. **Planner spike** — does a fresh-context planner produce useful AC checklists on 3 real tickets, including one "unsliceable" one? Output: written recommendation; decision to proceed/reshape/kill before slice 2.
120	2. **shape:design-doc** — architecture for the required-skill-sequence contract: label mechanism, prompt structure, halt behaviour, run-log schema, OTel attribute shape, decision §1 supersession path.
121	3. **`flow:` label mechanism** — Linear label group, parser, validation; mirrors `repo:` / `model:` resolution from decisions §5 and §7. Default = no flow (current behaviour).
122	4. **Run-log schema extension** — new top-level + per-entry fields for `skill_sequence_trace`, `planner_verdict`, `verifier_verdict`, `flow_label`, `halt_reason` if applicable. OTel span attributes extended in parallel.
123	5–6. **Worker integration: planner pre-step** — invoke `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` as first action when `flow:plan-implement-verify` is set; capture AC checklist; halt if planner can't produce one.
124	7–8. **Worker integration: verifier post-step** — invoke `/code-review-and-quality` against working-tree changes; parse verdict; gate Done transition.
125	9. **Halt-on-verifier-fail + revert path** — extends decision §8/§9 machinery: leaves worktree, halts cycle, names breach.
126	10. **`drain-cycle grade` extensions** — `contract_pass_count`, `trace_complete_rate`, silent-Done detection.
127	11. **Cost-impact handling** — measure per-flow token multiplier; add `flow:` overrides in `limits.yml` if cycle cap pressure shows up.
128	12. **`docs/design-decisions.md` §1 supersession** — write a successor decision explaining the verifier-gated Done contract; mark §1 superseded with link.
129	
130	## §8 Kill condition
131	
132	If, after running contract-enabled flows on **5+ complex tickets across 2 cycles**, the verifier produces **zero true-positive findings** (every halt was a false positive, and verifier-passed Dones don't visibly improve PR-review outcomes vs non-contract drains) — the planner+verifier overhead isn't earning its keep.
133	
134	Action on kill: revert the contract enforcement (the `flow:` label becomes a no-op or is removed); keep the instrumentation (skill-sequence trace stays in run log + OTel); record the negative result as a new decision in `docs/design-decisions.md`.
135	
136	**Open question:** is "operator-touch events per cycle > 2" (the §6.3.B target) also a kill trigger, or only a "tune the contract" trigger? If yes, append to this condition.
137	
138	## §9 Out of scope (explicitly)
139	
140	- **PR feedback loop on stacked Graphite PRs** — sibling initiative. Shares skill-as-primitive philosophy but has its own trigger problem and blast radius.
141	- **Parallel drains** — orthogonal; correctness-focused initiative has no reason to touch decision §8's serial-drain stance.
142	- **Authoring new skills** in `agent-skills-shaper` — initiative reuses existing skills; new-skill authoring would be a separate initiative if needed.
143	
144	## §10 Open review points (summary)
145	
146	1. **§6.3 — KR3 slot:** A (foundation), B (maintenance, blocked by rule 5b), or C (both, with 4 KRs, also blocked unless one dimension reclassified). Recommendation: **A**.
147	2. **§6.1 — KR1 grading:** is "no operator-required fix" verified manually, or via a stricter automated proxy (e.g. zero post-drain operator commits)?
148	3. **§6.2 — KR2 measurement window:** "every contract-enabled cycle for the initiative's duration" — fine, or should it cap (e.g. first 10 cycles) for grading discipline?
149	4. **§7 — appetite shape:** ~12 issues feels right; any slice you'd drop or split?
150	5. **§8 — kill condition:** should operator-touch volume be a kill trigger or only a tuning trigger?
151	6. **§9 — out-of-scope:** anything in here that should actually be in scope, or anything in-scope above that you'd push out?
152	
153	## §11 Next steps
154	
155	1. You leave inline feedback on this doc.
156	2. I revise §6 and any other affected sections.
157	3. Repeat until you confirm (Step 6 gate).
158	4. I run the 11-rule verification rubric (Step 6.5) and report any failures.
159	5. Once all 11 rules pass, I create the Linear project via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_project` with the synthesised body, status Planned.
160	6. Sibling work (the design-doc, slice 2 of §7) becomes the next phase.
161	
```

The skill caps KRs at 5 and warns against padding, so we should commit to a clean 3-KR mix unless a 4th dimension is genuinely load-bearing.\n\n#### §6.3 — Recommendation\n\n**Lean toward Option A (foundation).** Reasons:\n1. KR1 isn't gradable without the trace — the foundation is structurally required.\n2. KR2 already covers the no-silent-failure property that Type 2's playbook defaults to.\n3. Maintenance-burden as a target can live in the **kill condition** (§8) rather than as a KR — \"if the contract adds > 2 operator-touch events per cycle, kill it\" — which is honest about it being a stopping condition rather than a thing we're tracking weekly.\n\n**Open question for review:** confirm A, or push back if you actually want B/C.",
  "new_string": "## §6 Goal and Key results\n\nThe KRs below are written in plain English — a non-pack-author should be able to grade either one in 30 seconds. Rubric annotations (Layer 1 / Layer 2 dimensions) live in italic audit-footers under each KR; the dimensions summary is at the end of §6.\n\n### §6.0 Goal\n\n**For** the operator running drain-cycle on complex tickets that can't be sliced further, **we want** a second agent to verify the produced code before the ticket is marked Done — **so that** incomplete or under-verified work doesn't silently land on `main`.\n\n### §6.1 KR1 (stretch) — Of the next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow, ≥ 3 ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits *(bet)*\n\n- **plain-English version:** when I run drain-cycle on a hard ticket I couldn't break into smaller pieces, a second agent reviews the produced code before the ticket is marked Done. The bet is that this catches enough real problems that most PRs merge cleanly without me having to push fix commits.\n- **baseline:** 0 of 0 — no second-agent review exists today; the worker marks the ticket Done itself (per design decision §1). The ship-rate-without-fixes is unmeasured because there's no record of which tickets went through which path.\n- **target:** ≥ 3 of the next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits.\n- **measured over:** the first 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow (~2 cycles at current cadence).\n- **how we'll grade it:** two new artefacts, both built as slice 10 in §7:\n  1. **Per-ticket grade template** — a markdown checklist filled in after each drain that used the new flow. Questions: did the code cover every stated AC? did the review catch any real issue? did the PR merge clean, or did I push fix commits? Stored at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md`, one file per graded ticket.\n  2. **`drain-cycle grade --flow=verify`** — extends the existing per-cycle grade command (which already reads `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*.json`); reads the per-ticket grade files and reports pass-count and pass-rate across a window of tickets.\n\n  **What \"new\" actually means here** (re c_b1b472): no new streaming logs or metrics are emitted — drain-cycle already writes a run-log JSON per invocation and (since commit 3eae2f5) OTel spans to Honeycomb. The new things are (a) **two new fields** added to each run-log entry — `flow` (which flow this drain used; `null` for the default flow) and `verifier_verdict` (the reviewer agent's pass/fail and findings); (b) **two new OTel span attributes** mirroring those fields on the `drain.issue` span; (c) **the per-ticket grade markdown** described above; (d) **the `--flow` subcommand** on the existing `drain-cycle grade`.\n\n*Layer 1: correctness · Layer 2: golden-path test*\n\n### §6.2 KR2 (commit) — Every ticket reaching Done in Linear under the new flow has a recorded second-agent review verdict in the run log; zero exceptions *(brake)*\n\n- **plain-English version:** the worker is no longer trusted to mark its own ticket Done. The contract is: a ticket using the new flow gets marked Done only if the run log shows a second-agent review ran and produced a verdict. This protects against the failure mode where the agent thinks it's finished but actually missed half the AC.\n- **baseline:** silent self-assertion currently possible (per decision §1's contract); count of past silent Dones is unknown because the recording fields don't exist yet.\n- **target:** 0 tickets reach Done in Linear under the new flow without a `verifier_verdict` recorded in the run log.\n- **measured over:** every cycle that runs the new flow, for the initiative's duration (cap at 10 cycles if the initiative runs long).\n- **how we'll grade it:** `drain-cycle grade` is extended to fail any entry where `flow == \"verify\"` AND `final_linear_state == \"completed\"` AND `verifier_verdict` is absent. Exit code ≠ 0 on any violation. A single violation in any cycle fails the KR — this is a brake, not a rate.\n\n*Layer 1: maintenance · Layer 2: structural cap + revisit gate*\n\n### §6.3 KR count — manual override taken\n\n**KR3 dropped per review** (c_a2a5ee). The instrumentation work it would have measured (per-agent step records in the run log and OTel trace) is folded into KR1's \"how we'll grade it\" as a prerequisite, and remains explicitly scoped as slice 4 of §7. Keeping it as a separate KR was padding given how tightly coupled it is to KR1's grading mechanism — you can't grade KR1 without the instrumentation, so KR1's how-we'll-know already mandates it.\n\nThis takes the operator's offered manual override of rule 4 (default ≥ 3 KRs). The 11-rule rubric at Step 6.5 will flag rule 4 as failed; the override note in this section is the documented justification.\n\n*Dimensions: correctness / maintenance — distinct per rule 5b. KR count = 2 (manual override per review c_a2a5ee — instrumentation work survives as slice 4 of §7, not as a separate KR).*"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md",
  "old_string": "10. **Grading workflow** — (a) per-ticket grade template at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` matching KR1's questions; (b) `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` subcommand reading the templates and reporting pass-count / pass-rate across a ticket window; (c) silent-Done detection (KR2 grader): fail any run-log entry where `flow == \"verify\"` AND `final_linear_state == \"completed\"` AND `verifier_verdict` is absent.",
  "new_string": "10. **Grading workflow** — designed for low-friction operator confirmation rather than blank-page authorship (per round-2 review c_1d55a4):\n    1. **Per-ticket grade markdown template** at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` — checklist matching KR1's questions plus a `status:` field (`draft` / `confirmed`).\n    2. **Auto-draft on completion** — when a contract-enabled drain finishes, drain-cycle invokes a one-shot grading agent (single `claude -p` call) with the ticket body + run-log entry + diff, and writes a `status: draft` file at the template path with the agent's first-pass answers. Operator opens the file, reviews, edits where the agent got it wrong, flips `status: confirmed`. Less friction than authoring blank; still operator-owned at the moment of judgement.\n    3. **`drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` subcommand** — reads grade files with `status: confirmed`, reports pass-count and pass-rate across a ticket window; warns about un-confirmed drafts so they don't get forgotten.\n    4. **Silent-Done detection** (KR2 grader): fail any run-log entry where `flow == \"verify\"` AND `final_linear_state == \"completed\"` AND `verifier_verdict` is absent."
}
```

130	
131	Each KR's claim (its headline) should be one of: a binary pass/fail ("X works on the common path with no manual intervention"), a fitness function firing ("the guard fails the run loudly when Y"), or a measurable delta ("token footprint drops vs baseline"). No "improve X" / "better Y" / "run N times" language. Every KR must have all four sub-fields (baseline / target / measured over / how we'll know), a `(commit|stretch)` tag, and a `bet`/`brake`/`foundation` role appended to the headline. Add the audit-footer line (`*Layer 1: … · Layer 2: …*`) under each KR, and the dimensions-summary line after the KR list — this is the rubric trace, kept out of the KR body so the OKR reads cleanly to a non-pack-author.
132	
133	Present the draft. Do not create the Linear project yet.
134	
135	**6. [GATE] User confirms the draft.**
136	Ask explicitly: "Does this capture the initiative correctly? Any changes to the Objective, the Key results, kill condition, or project type before I create the project?" Do not proceed until confirmed. Fixing a wrong problem statement, vague KR, or missing kill condition here takes one minute; fixing it mid-cycle costs days.
137	
138	**6.5. [GATE] Verification rubric — 11 cross-cutting rules.**
139	Run the following checks against the confirmed draft. Each rule is a binary pass/fail. Any failure returns the draft to Step 5 for repair — the Linear project is NOT created until all 11 pass.
140	
141	| # | Rule | Pass condition |
142	|---|---|---|
143	| 1 | All six fields present | Goal / Key results / Affected repos / Appetite / Kill condition / Project type are all non-empty |
144	| 2 | Goal names who + outcome | Sentence contains an affected party and a desired outcome — no solution verb ("build", "add", "integrate", "ship") |
145	| 3 | Initiative name is a goal/problem | Name does not open with a solution verb ("Build X", "Add Y", "Integrate Z") |
146	| 4 | At least 3 KRs, no more than 5 | KR count ≥ 3 and ≤ 5 |
147	| 5a | No degenerate KRs | `target ≠ baseline` either numerically (different number) or by state change (different observable state) — bans "baseline 100%, target 100%" patterns |
148	| 5b | KRs name distinct Layer 1 dimensions | Across all KRs, no two share the same Layer 1 dimension. Default vocabulary: correctness / outcome / maintenance / discipline (see `references/kr-quality-templates.md`). An out-of-default dimension (e.g. performance) is permitted only when the initiative genuinely has that property |
149	| 5c | KRs are not Goal-restatements | Strip the Goal to its measurable noun-phrase; no KR's observable state matches it. Each KR must name a *measurable property of* the Goal — not the Goal itself |
150	| 5d | KR matches its Layer 2 template | For each KR, name which Layer 1 dimension it sits in and confirm it matches the template (golden-path test / self-trial protocol / structural cap + revisit gate / artefact-exists + sections-complete) in `references/kr-quality-templates.md` |
151	| 5e | Language ban | No KR contains "improve", "better", "enhance", or arbitrary "run N times" count thresholds without a correctness or quality dimension |
152	| 5f | KR is grader-backed (or carved out with justification) | Each KR's `how we'll know:` field points at a command / script / query that emits a verdict against the target — describable in one sentence at draft time. Manual grading is permitted only under the carve-out: (a) the initiative is genuinely one-shot or throwaway, OR (b) the verdict is irreducibly qualitative ("the prose reads naturally", "the diagram is legible") and no honest automatable proxy exists. The carve-out must be stated alongside the KR. Building the grader (write-side schema + read-side command) is scoped into the initiative as one or two user stories before it enters Active. See `references/kr-quality-templates.md` "The grader-backed KR pattern" |
153	| 6 | Every KR has all four sub-fields, no placeholders, `how we'll know` is a concrete artefact | Each KR has baseline, target, measured over, how we'll know — none blank, none equal to `TBD`, `unknown`, `currently unknown`, `we'll measure it`, `we'll figure it out`, `various`, `the logs`, `the system`, `the dashboard`, `better than today`, `the report`. `how we'll know` points to a concrete artefact: a file path, a directory, a Linear query, a log query (e.g. `bigquery: events WHERE ...`), a cached report URL, or a metric name + system |
154	| 7 | Every KR is tagged | Each KR carries exactly one of `(commit)` or `(stretch)` |
155	| 8 | Kill condition is present | Non-empty; names an observable state, not a vague clause ("if it doesn't work") |
156	| 9 | Project type is canonical | One of: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, or 6 — not free text |
157	| 10 | Appetite is in issues | Expressed as "~N issues" — not days, weeks, or sprints |
158	| 11 | Every KR has a role tag | Each KR carries exactly one of `bet` / `brake` / `foundation` — roles defined in `references/kr-quality-templates.md` "Roles within an initiative" |
159	
160	**Soft check (warning, not a hard fail):** if every KR is tagged `bet`, surface a warning — most initiatives have at most 1–2 bets, and an all-bet draft usually means the brakes and foundation were silently omitted. This does not block project creation, but name it to the user and ask whether a brake (something that already works and shouldn't regress) or foundation (instrumentation the bet depends on to be measurable) KR is missing. Do not force one of each role — some shapes are honestly 2 bets + 1 foundation, or 1 bet + 2 brakes.
161	
162	Report each failing rule by number with the specific text that triggered the failure. For rule 5, name the specific sub-rule (5a / 5b / 5c / 5d / 5e / 5f) that failed, so the user knows whether to fix the baseline-equals-target problem (5a), the duplicate-dimension problem (5b), the Goal-restatement problem (5c), the template-mismatch problem (5d), the language problem (5e), or the missing-grader problem (5f). When all checks pass, proceed to Step 7.
163	
164	**7. Create the Linear project.**
165	Call `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_project` with:
166	- `name`: goal or problem label — not a solution name, not a repo name
167	- `description`: the six-field initiative format (see template below)
168	- Status: Planned

174	
175	## Initiative description template
176	
177	The output is the Linear project description. It must read as a product OKR — a non-pack-author should be able to grade each KR in 30 seconds. Each KR's headline *is* the observable claim (there is no separate `state:` field). The four sub-fields use PM-readable names; the rubric vocabulary lives only in the italic audit-footer under each KR and the dimensions-summary line after the list — never in the KR body. The headline ends with a PM-readable `*(bet|brake|foundation)*` role tag (the push / a guardrail / an enabler) — plain English, so it stays on the headline, unlike the Layer 1/Layer 2 rubric vocabulary that lives in the footer.

1	# Draft Linear initiative — multi-agent collaboration for correctness
2	
3	**Status:** draft for review (round 4 reshape; Step 5 of `initiative-shape`). Linear project NOT created yet — pending operator confirmation (Step 6) and the 11-rule verification rubric (Step 6.5).
4	
5	**How to use this doc:** leave inline feedback on any section. The roster in §1, the KRs in §6, and the milestone structure in §7 are the primary review surfaces.
6	
7	---
8	
9	## §1 Raw idea (Step 1)
10	
11	drain-cycle evolves from "drain a Linear cycle unattended" toward a **UNIX-philosophy dispatcher for composable skill primitives** — Linear tickets as the work queue, skills+MCPs as the small tools, and `drain-cycle` itself as the batch-mode shell that composes them. Operator (you) drives the *why* (initiative goal + KRs) and the *what* (outcome-focused Linear issue); agents handle the *how* through a defined set of skills. The same skills are invokable manually at the keyboard OR automatically inside a worker session — design decision §10's symlink already makes this true.
12	
13	The operator failure mode this initiative addresses: **complex tickets that can't be sliced further** where the worker forgets details, under-verifies, or submits an unstructured PR — followed by human review comments that pile up while the operator manually re-invokes the agent to address each round.
14	
15	The bet: six agent-driven roles end-to-end, each backed by a skill (or CLI subcommand) that fits the UNIX-philosophy frame:
16	
17	| # | Role | Phase | Skill / CLI | Status |
18	|---|---|---|---|---|
19	| 0 | **Operator** (you) | Throughout | Operator-side skill table; defines *why* + *what* | — |
20	| 1 | **Task Shaper** | Before code | `/shape:outcome-tasks` *(refined `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`)* | [R] |
21	| 2 | **Implementer** | Middle | 6 existing skills (incremental-implementation, test-driven-development, source-driven-development, code-review-and-quality, git-workflow-and-versioning, debugging-and-error-recovery) | [E] |
22	| 3 | **Outcome Verifier** | After implementation, before Done | `/shape:outcome-verify` *(new)* | [N] |
23	| 4 | **PR Preparer** | After Outcome Verifier passes, before PR submission | `/shape:pr-prepare` *(new)* | [N] |
24	| 5 | **PR Responder** | On new human review comments (operator-launched polling loop) | `/shape:pr-respond` *(new)*, launched via `drain-cycle pr-feedback` subcommand | [N] |
25	| 6 | **Grader** (post-drain) | After worker session finishes | `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` CLI subcommand | [—] |
26	
27	Roles 1 and 3 are a **complementary pair**: `/shape:outcome-tasks` defines the outcome-shaped task list at the start; `/shape:outcome-verify` validates the outcome at the end. Roles 4 and 5 are the **PR-side pair**: Preparer shapes outbound PRs, Responder addresses inbound review feedback. Role 6 is operator-confirm scaffolding for grading the initiative against its KRs.
28	
29	**Constraints carried from prior rounds:**
30	- Subscription auth only (Claude Code / Codex CLI subprocess) — no API-key models. GitHub webhooks themselves are free, but the agent itself can't run inside GitHub Actions; agent work has to happen on the operator's machine.
31	- Correctness > throughput. No parallel drains. No throughput-focused changes.
32	
33	## §2 Problem-or-solution gate (Step 2)
34	
35	**Passes.** The operator-impacting failures are named and specific:
36	
37	1. **Silent Done on hard tickets.** Complex unsliceable tickets get drained; worker self-asserts Done; produced diff misses AC; operator finds out at PR review and fixes by hand.
38	2. **Unstructured PR submission.** PRs land without What/Why/Focus guidance; reviewer has to guess what to look at, or the operator manually rewrites the body.
39	3. **Stalled feedback loops.** Human review comments accumulate on stacked PRs; operator manually re-invokes claude to address each round.
40	
41	The six-role roster is the proposed solution; each role maps to one of the failure modes (Task Shaper + Outcome Verifier address #1; PR Preparer addresses #2; PR Responder addresses #3).
42	
43	## §3 Project type (Step 2.5)
44	
45	**Type 2 — personal product, single-user.** Confirmed.
46	
47	Rationale: drain-cycle is described in `AGENTS.md` as "Python, single-user, personal product"; the artefact being evolved is the product itself; any new skills/MCPs are primitives drain-cycle invokes, not the headline deliverable; one user (you), so growth/MAU KRs are categorically inapplicable per Type 2 playbook.
48	
49	## §4 Negative outcome if not solved
50	
51	If the initiative is not built:
52	
53	- **Hard tickets continue to ship Done with missing AC.** The worker self-asserts Done (per design decision §1); the only feedback is at PR-review time, when the operator either accepts the gap or pushes manual fix commits. The unattended-drain promise breaks on exactly the tickets where it matters most.
54	- **PR review burden falls entirely on the human.** Reviewers (operator + collaborators) open PRs with no guidance on what to focus on; auto-mergeable changes get the same scrutiny as risky ones; review throughput is slower than it needs to be.
55	- **Human review feedback creates a manual re-invocation tax.** Each round of comments on a stacked PR requires the operator to context-switch back into the worktree, re-launch claude, and shepherd the agent through the fix. The agent ecosystem stops at code-submission time instead of running end-to-end through merge.
56	
57	## §5 Affected repos
58	
59	- **`drain-cycle`** — primary. Worker integration of roles 1–5, run-log schema extensions, `verify` label mechanism, CLI subcommands (`grade-draft`, `grade --flow=verify`), supersession of design decision §1, OTel attribute additions.
60	- **`agent-skills-shaper`** — secondary, conditional. Receives one refined skill (`/shape:outcome-tasks`, refined from `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown`) and three new skills (`/shape:outcome-verify`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, `/shape:pr-respond`). Open decision C in §10 controls whether new-skill authoring happens inside this initiative or splits to a sibling.
61	
62	## §6 Goal and Key results
63	
64	The KRs are written in plain English — a non-pack-author should be able to grade each in 30 seconds. Rubric annotations (Layer 1 / Layer 2 dimensions) live in italic audit-footers under each KR.
65	
66	### §6.0 Goal
67	
68	**For** the operator running drain-cycle on outcome-focused Linear tickets, **we want** six agent-driven roles that take work from ticket to merged PR — task-shaping before code, outcome verification before Done, structured PR preparation, and autonomous response to human review comments — **so that** complex tickets don't silently land with missing AC, PRs are reviewable without guesswork, and human review feedback doesn't create a manual re-invocation tax.
69	
70	### §6.1 KR1 (stretch) — Of the next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow, ≥ 3 ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits *(bet)*
71	
72	- **plain-English version:** when I run drain-cycle on a hard ticket I couldn't break into smaller pieces, six agent roles take it from intake to merged PR. The bet is that this catches enough real problems and structures the PRs well enough that most merge cleanly without me having to push fix commits.
73	- **baseline:** 0 of 0 — none of the six roles exist as a connected flow today.
74	- **target:** ≥ 3 of the next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits.
75	- **measured over:** the first 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow (~2 cycles at current cadence).
76	- **how we'll grade it:** per-ticket grade markdown at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` (auto-drafted by `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>`, operator confirms `draft` → `confirmed`); `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` reports the rolled-up pass-rate across the window.
77	
78	*Layer 1: correctness · Layer 2: golden-path test*
79	
80	### §6.2 KR2 (commit) — Every ticket reaching Done in Linear under the new flow has a recorded Outcome Verifier verdict in the run log; zero exceptions *(brake)*
81	
82	- **plain-English version:** the worker is no longer trusted to mark its own ticket Done. The contract is: a ticket using the new flow gets marked Done only if the run log shows the Outcome Verifier ran and produced a verdict. Protects against the failure mode where the agent thinks it's finished but actually missed half the AC.
83	- **baseline:** silent self-assertion currently possible (per design decision §1); count of past silent Dones is unknown because the recording fields don't exist yet.
84	- **target:** 0 tickets reach Done in Linear under the new flow without an `outcome_verdict` recorded in the run log.
85	- **measured over:** every cycle that runs the new flow, for the initiative's duration (cap at 10 cycles if the initiative runs long).
86	- **how we'll grade it:** `drain-cycle grade` is extended to fail any entry where `flow == "verify"` AND `final_linear_state == "completed"` AND `outcome_verdict` is absent. Exit code ≠ 0 on any violation. A single violation in any cycle fails the KR — this is a brake, not a rate.
87	
88	*Layer 1: maintenance · Layer 2: structural cap + revisit gate*
89	
90	### §6.3 KR3 (commit) — Every PR submitted under the new flow has the structured What / Why / Focus body, or is correctly routed to auto-merge *(brake)*
91	
92	- **plain-English version:** the PR Preparer either writes a structured What / Why / Focus body and routes the PR for human review, or it correctly identifies the PR as auto-mergeable (no critical focus items) and routes it to auto-merge. Either way, every PR comes out with a documented routing decision and a reviewable body.
93	- **baseline:** today, PRs from drained tickets have whatever body the worker produced — typically a one-line subject and no review guidance.
94	- **target:** 0 PRs submitted under the new flow without either (a) structured What/Why/Focus body OR (b) auto-merge label with PR Preparer reasoning attached.
95	- **measured over:** every PR submitted under the new flow, for the initiative's duration.
96	- **how we'll grade it:** PR Preparer writes its decision to a `prep_verdict` field on the run-log entry; `drain-cycle grade` fails any verify-flow entry where `prep_verdict` is absent or where the verdict says "structured body" but the actual PR body is empty/single-line.
97	
98	*Layer 1: discipline · Layer 2: artefact-exists + sections-complete*
99	
100	### §6.4 KR rule check
101	
102	Rule 4 (≥ 3 KRs) passes: three KRs covering three distinct failure modes (correctness, maintenance discipline, PR-shape discipline). Rule 5b (distinct dimensions) passes: correctness / maintenance / discipline. No override needed.
103	
104	The PR Responder (role 5) doesn't get its own KR — its existence is structurally captured by KR1 (clean-merge rate would degrade if the responder doesn't address review feedback). Adding a fourth KR for responder-specific metrics would push toward padding given KR1 already exercises that surface end-to-end.
105	
106	## §7 Appetite
107	
108	**~18–22 issues across five milestones.** Each milestone has a review gate at the end — the operator reviews progress and either continues to the next milestone or pauses for a reshape/kill decision if something clear has come up. Default at each gate is review-and-continue, not stop-the-line (per round-7 c_8ebf0b). Whole-initiative kill conditions are separate (§8).
109	
110	### M1 — Recording infrastructure
111	
112	Build the data layer first, behaviour later.
113	
114	- `verify` Linear label parser added alongside the existing `repo:` / `model:` resolution in `labels.py`.
115	- Run-log schema fields: `flow` (string, e.g. `"verify"`, or `null` for default flow), `outcome_verdict` (object: `{result, findings[], invoked_at}` or `null`), `prep_verdict` (object: `{result, route, reasoning}` or `null`), `responder_runs[]` (array of objects: `{comment_ids[], invoked_at, result}` — appended each time the responder runs).
116	- Matching attributes on the `drain.issue` OTel span.
117	- `docs/design-decisions.md` §1 supersession: write the successor decision explaining the verifier-gated Done contract; mark §1 as superseded.
118	
119	**Gate:** an end-to-end `verify`-labelled ticket drains; new fields populate (all as `null` since no roles are wired yet); existing behaviour unchanged.
120	
121	### M2 — Worker-internal loop (Task Shaper, Outcome Verifier, halt path)
122	
123	- **Task Shaper** (`/shape:outcome-tasks`): refined skill that takes a Linear issue and produces enriched AC checklist + sizing decision (single stack vs N stacks) + per-stack task list. Open decision B in §10 governs whether this is a full reshape of `/shape:planning-and-task-breakdown` or a narrower variant alongside.
124	- **Where the sizing decision lands** (per round-7 c_3a5c84): dual-write. (a) Fed into the implementer in-session as context; (b) appended to the **Linear issue body** so future resumes / pickups have the full context without re-running the Task Shaper. The Linear write is the durable artefact.
125	- **Implementer integration**: worker session pulls the Task Shaper output as context; no other implementer changes.
126	- **Outcome Verifier** (`/shape:outcome-verify`): new skill. Inputs: ticket body + Task Shaper output + diff + run-log. Output: structured verdict (pass / fail + findings) written to `outcome_verdict`.
127	- **Halt-on-fail path**: extends decision §8 / §9 machinery — on Outcome Verifier fail, leave worktree, halt cycle, name the breach in `cycle_halt_reason`.
128	
129	**Gate:** 3 verify-labelled hard tickets drained; verdicts recorded; halt path exercised at least once on a deliberate failure (e.g. a synthetic ticket whose AC the implementer can't satisfy).
130	
131	### M3 — PR-shape gate (PR Preparer)
132	
133	- **PR Preparer** (`/shape:pr-prepare`): new skill. Reads stack PRs via Graphite MCP; writes What / Why / Focus body on each; decides auto-merge vs human-review per the carve-out rule (small + no human review needed → auto-merge; otherwise → human-review).
134	- **Auto-merge mechanism**: defaults to Graphite-native (`gt submit --merge-when-ready`-equivalent). Open decision F in §10 covers the alternative (`gh pr merge --auto`).
135	- Records decision to `prep_verdict` on the run-log entry.
136	
137	**Gate:** 3 stacks emitted, all with structured What / Why / Focus bodies or auto-merge labels. PR-body assertion in `drain-cycle grade` passes on all three.
138	
139	### M4 — PR-feedback loop (PR Responder)
140	
141	- **PR Responder** (`/shape:pr-respond`): new skill. Reads new review comments on stack PRs via Graphite MCP; makes the fix in the right worktree; pushes to the stack; appends to `responder_runs[]`.
142	- **Trigger model**: a separate operator-launched subcommand — `drain-cycle pr-feedback` (per rp_c4bc10). The operator launches it explicitly; the command then runs as a polling loop, addressing PR comments as they arrive, until the operator stops it. Not invoked implicitly at drain-start, not a GitHub Action, not background-daemon-on-boot. Foreground polling agent the operator owns the lifetime of.
143	- **Idempotency**: each comment ID tracked in `responder_runs[].comment_ids[]` so the same comment isn't addressed twice across `pr-feedback` invocations.
144	
145	**Gate:** one real PR with operator-left review feedback gets addressed end-to-end by the responder; fix lands on the stack; run-log shows the responder run.
146	
147	### M5 — Grading + cost + close-out
148	
149	- **Per-ticket grade template** at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` with checklist + `status:` field.
150	- **`drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` CLI subcommand**: one-shot grading prompt. Invoked automatically on drain completion (writes `status: draft`); also invokable manually by the operator for retro-fill on past tickets.
151	- **`drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` subcommand**: reads `confirmed` grade files, reports pass-rate across a ticket window, warns about un-confirmed drafts.
152	- **Silent-Done detection** (KR2 grader): exit-code-nonzero on any `flow == "verify"` AND `final_linear_state == "completed"` AND `outcome_verdict == null` entry.
153	- **Cost-impact handling**: measure per-ticket token multiplier under the new flow; add `verify`-flow-specific overrides in `limits.yml` if cycle cap pressure shows up.
154	- **Post-launch review**: write a Linear comment on each KR's status at end of the initiative window; either ship cleanly, pause, or kill.
155	
156	**Gate:** KR grading runs end-to-end; one of {ship cleanly, pause, kill} is decided and recorded.
157	
158	## §8 Kill condition
159	
160	**Primary kill (whole-initiative):** after running the new flow on 5+ hard tickets across 2 cycles, the Outcome Verifier produces zero true-positive findings (every halt was a false positive, and verifier-passed Dones don't visibly improve PR-review outcomes vs default-flow drains). Carried from round-3.
161	
162	**Secondary concerns (watched at each milestone's review gate, not auto-kills per round-7 c_8ebf0b):**
163	
164	- M1 — schema additions cause unanticipated breakage in existing run-log readers or downstream tooling.
165	- M3 — PR Preparer's bodies don't measurably reduce reviewer cognitive load (signal: operator subjectively prefers the raw worker-produced PRs).
166	- M4 — PR Responder either (a) can't address comments cleanly without operator intervention >50% of the time, or (b) the polling loop drops or misses comments in practice.
167	
168	Any of these surfacing at a milestone review prompts a discussion (continue / reshape / kill), but the default is continue. Only the primary kill above is automatic.
169	
170	**Action on kill:** revert the contract enforcement (the `verify` label becomes a no-op or is removed); keep the recording fields — they stay in run log + OTel because they're cheap and useful for future investigation; record the negative result as a new decision in `docs/design-decisions.md`.
171	
172	## §9 Out of scope (explicitly)
173	
174	**Moves into scope (vs round-3):** PR Preparer (M3); PR Responder / feedback loop (M4).
175	
176	**Stays out of scope:**
177	- **Parallel drains.** Decision §8's serial-drain stance unchanged.
178	- **Replacing Graphite.** The PR-side work assumes the existing Graphite stack flow; switching PR-management tools is a separate decision.
179	- **Multi-operator / multi-tenant workflows.** Single-user product per §3.
180

| # | Rule | Result | Evidence |

| 5d | KR matches its Layer 2 template | ✅ | KR1 golden-path test · KR2 structural cap + revisit gate · KR3 artefact-exists + sections-complete |

| 5f | KR is grader-backed | ✅ | KR1 → `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` over `~/.drain-cycle/grades/*.md`; KR2 → `drain-cycle grade` silent-Done check; KR3 → `prep_verdict` field check. Grader scaffolding scoped into M5 |

KR2's "count of past silent Dones is unknown" is a stated *why-no-numeric-baseline*, not a `TBD` placeholder; the state baseline ("silent self-assertion currently possible") is concrete |
| 7 | Every KR has `(commit)` or `(stretch)` | ✅ | KR1 stretch · KR2 commit · KR3 commit |

| 9 | Project type canonical | ✅ | Type 2 |

## Step 7 — proposed Linear project body

Synthesised against the template in §"Initiative description template" of the skill. Before I call `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_project`, here is the exact `description` I'd write — please scan for last-minute edits:

```markdown
**Goal:** For the operator running drain-cycle on outcome-focused Linear
tickets, we want six agent-driven roles that take work from ticket to
merged PR — task-shaping before code, outcome verification before Done,
structured PR preparation, and autonomous response to human review
comments — so that complex tickets don't silently land with missing AC,
PRs are reviewable without guesswork, and human review feedback doesn't
create a manual re-invocation tax.

**Key results:**

**KR1 (stretch)** — Of the next 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow,
≥ 3 ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits *(bet)*
- baseline: 0 of 0 — none of the six roles exist as a connected flow today
- target: ≥ 3 of 5 ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits
- measured over: the first 5 hard tickets drained with the new flow
  (~2 cycles at current cadence)
- how we'll know: per-ticket grade markdown at
  `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` (auto-drafted by
  `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>`, operator confirms draft → confirmed);
  `drain-cycle grade --flow=verify` reports the rolled-up pass-rate.

*Layer 1: correctness · Layer 2: golden-path test*

**KR2 (commit)** — Every ticket reaching Done in Linear under the new flow
has a recorded Outcome Verifier verdict in the run log; zero exceptions
*(brake)*
- baseline: silent self-assertion currently possible (per design decision
  §1); past count unknown because recording fields don't exist yet
- target: 0 tickets reach Done in Linear under the new flow without an
  `outcome_verdict` recorded in the run log
- measured over: every cycle that runs the new flow, capped at 10 cycles
- how we'll know: `drain-cycle grade` fails any entry where
  `flow == "verify"` AND `final_linear_state == "completed"` AND
  `outcome_verdict` is absent; exit code ≠ 0 on any violation

*Layer 1: maintenance · Layer 2: structural cap + revisit gate*

**KR3 (commit)** — Every PR submitted under the new flow has the structured
What / Why / Focus body, or is correctly routed to auto-merge *(brake)*
- baseline: PRs from drained tickets have whatever body the worker
  produced — typically a one-line subject and no review guidance
- target: 0 PRs submitted under the new flow without either (a) structured
  What/Why/Focus body OR (b) auto-merge label with PR Preparer reasoning
- measured over: every PR submitted under the new flow, for the
  initiative's duration
- how we'll know: PR Preparer writes its decision to `prep_verdict` on the
  run-log entry; `drain-cycle grade` fails any verify-flow entry where
  `prep_verdict` is absent or where the verdict says "structured body" but
  the actual PR body is empty/single-line

*Layer 1: discipline · Layer 2: artefact-exists + sections-complete*

*Dimensions: correctness / maintenance / discipline — all distinct per rule 5b.*

**Affected repos:** drain-cycle (primary) · agent-skills-shaper (secondary,
for one refined skill + three new skills).

**Appetite:** ~18–22 issues across five milestones (M1 recording infra ·
M2 worker-internal loop · M3 PR-shape gate · M4 PR-feedback loop ·
M5 grading + cost + close-out). Each milestone has a review-and-continue
gate; primary kill is whole-initiative only.

**Kill condition:** after running the new flow on 5+ hard tickets across
2 cycles, the Outcome Verifier produces zero true-positive findings
(every halt was a false positive, and verifier-passed Dones don't
visibly improve PR-review outcomes vs default-flow drains). On kill:
revert contract enforcement, keep recording fields, record negative
result in `docs/design-decisions.md`.

**Project type:** 2 — personal product, single-user.

---

Source-of-truth shaping doc: `docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md`.
```
